\displaystyle\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{1}\{x\in V_{i}\}\widehat{T}_{i},\qquad\widehat{\gamma}_{0}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}\delta_{X_{i}}.

(107)

Then

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0,n}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0}(x)=\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}.

(108)

Moreover, there exist constants
C_{\mathrm{mass}}>0
and

C_{\mathrm{log}}\coloneqq e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}/\kappa
, depending only on

M_{\mu},M_{\nu},\|\zeta_{0}\|_{\infty},\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}
, such that the following bound holds almost surely:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}&\leq\frac{2C_{\Lambda}}{\kappa}\left(M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})+M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})\right)+\frac{2C_{\mathrm{mass}}}{\kappa}\left(|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|\right)\\
&\quad+2C_{\mathrm{log}}\bigl(|\log\alpha_{n}|+|\log\beta_{m}|\bigr)\\
&\quad-\frac{2}{\kappa}\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\widetilde{\mu}_{n})-\frac{2}{\kappa}\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\widetilde{\nu}_{m})\\
&\quad-\frac{2}{\kappa}\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}-\mu)-\frac{2}{\kappa}\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}-\nu)\end{aligned}

(109)

Consequently, taking expectations and using
Assumption
4
(in particular the a.s. lower bound
\widehat{M}_{\mu}\geq cM_{\mu}
), there exists a
constant
\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{mass}}>0
such that, for all sufficiently large
n,m
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{\Lambda}}{\kappa}\left(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]\right)+\frac{2\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{mass}}}{\kappa}\bigl(a_{n}+b_{m}\bigr).

(110)

The same bound therefore holds for the in-sample
1
NN error

\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0,n}^{\mathrm{1NN}}\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0}

by (
108
).

Proof of Proposition
17
.

The algebra in Proposition
14
does not use that the reference masses are the true masses.
Therefore, replacing

(\widetilde{\mu}_{n},\widetilde{\nu}_{m},\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{r},\widetilde{s},r^{\star},s^{\star})

by

(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m},\widehat{\gamma},\widehat{r},\widehat{s},\widehat{r}^{\star},\widehat{s}^{\star})

in that proof yields (
105
).

Lemma
2
and the nonnegativity of the discrete
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
terms in (
105
) imply

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{tr}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}.

(111)

Exactly the same Jensen argument as in Theorem
16
, now applied to the rows of
\widehat{\gamma}
, gives

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}\leq\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{tr}},

(112)

which proves (
106
).
The identity (
108
) is also the same computation as in Theorem
16
, with
\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}
replaced by
\widehat{\gamma}_{0}
.

Next, by duality and (
56
),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}\\
&\qquad=\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\nu)\\
&\qquad\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})+M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})\Bigr)+\bigl(|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|\bigr)\\
&\qquad\quad+e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}\bigl(|\log\alpha_{n}|+|\log\beta_{m}|\bigr)\\
&\qquad\quad-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\widetilde{\mu}_{n})-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\widetilde{\nu}_{m})\\
&\qquad\quad-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}-\nu),\end{aligned}

(113)

where we used
\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)=\int\zeta_{0}d\mu+\int\xi_{0}d\nu
and rearranged the linear-in-
(\widetilde{\mu}_{n},\widetilde{\nu}_{m})
terms produced by (
56
).
Since
\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\widetilde{\mu}_{n}
and
\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\widetilde{\nu}_{m}
are signed atomic measures carried by the observed supports,

\displaystyle\left|\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\widetilde{\mu}_{n})\right|\leq\|\zeta_{0}\|_{\infty}|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|,\qquad\left|\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\widetilde{\nu}_{m})\right|\leq\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|.

(114)

Substituting these bounds into (
113
), multiplying by
2/\kappa
via (
106
), and absorbing constants proves the bound (
109
).

Taking expectations of (
109
), the terms
\mathbb{E}[\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}-\mu)]
and
\mathbb{E}[\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}-\nu)]
vanish. By Assumption
4
,
\widehat{M}_{\mu}\geq cM_{\mu}
a.s. gives
|\log\alpha_{n}|\leq|\alpha_{n}-1|/c
a.s., hence
M_{\mu}\mathbb{E}[|\log\alpha_{n}|]\leq\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|]/c\leq a_{n}/c
, and analogously for the
\nu
-side, so

e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}\mathbb{E}[|\log\alpha_{n}|]\leq c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\nu}/M_{\mu}}a_{n}

and similarly
e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}\mathbb{E}[|\log\beta_{m}|]\leq c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\mu}/M_{\nu}}b_{m}
.
Setting
\widetilde{C}_{\mathrm{mass}}\coloneqq C_{\mathrm{mass}}+c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\mu}/M_{\nu}}+c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\nu}/M_{\mu}}
proves (
110
).
The last sentence is immediate from (
108
).
∎

E.2.
From the in-sample error to the population error

To pass from the in-sample barycentric error to the population error of the 1NN extension under
\gamma_{0}
, we compare the random Voronoi cell masses with the fitted row masses, keeping the row-marginal
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
error
{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widetilde{r}\mid r^{\star})
explicitly in the argument.

Lemma 18

(Bounds on
R_{n}
and
M_{n}
)
.

Let
V_{1},\dots,V_{n}
be the Voronoi cells induced by the source sample
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
, and define

\displaystyle M_{n}\coloneqq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\bar{\mu}(V_{i}),\qquad R_{n}\coloneqq\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sup_{x\in V_{i}}\|x-X_{i}\|.

Assume Assumptions
2
and
3
. Then there exist constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
, depending only on
d
,
\beta_{\min}
,
\beta_{\max}
,
\varepsilon_{0}
, and
\delta_{0}
, such that:

(i)

for every
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M_{n}\geq\frac{C_{1}}{n}\bigl[d\log n+\log(1/\delta)\bigr]\right)\leq\delta;

(115)

(ii)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{n}^{2}]\leq C_{2}\left({(\log n)/n}\right)^{2/d}.

(116)

Proof of Lemma
18
.

Let
\bar{p}
denote the density of
\bar{\mu}
.
Then
\bar{p}=p/M_{\mu}
is bounded above and below by positive constants on
\Omega
.
Moreover, Assumption
2
states exactly that
\Omega
is compact and satisfies the interior cone condition.
Hence the normalized sample
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\bar{\mu}
satisfies the hypotheses of
[
24
, Lemma 40]
.
Applying that lemma gives constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that, for every
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M_{n}\geq\frac{C_{1}}{n}\bigl[d\log n+\log(1/\delta)\bigr]\right)\leq\delta,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R_{n}^{2}]\leq C_{2}\left({(\log n)/n}\right)^{2/d}.

These are precisely (
115
) and (
116
).
∎

Lemma 19

(Bound on
\sum_{i}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\widehat{b}_{i}
)
.

Define

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2},\qquad\widehat{b}_{i}\coloneqq\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2},\qquad C_{\Omega}\coloneqq\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{2}.

Then, almost surely,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\widehat{b}_{i}\leq 3\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}+2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star}).

(117)

Proof of Lemma
19
.

Set

\displaystyle X_{n}^{\star}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\widehat{b}_{i},\qquad Y_{n}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}\widehat{b}_{i}=\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}.

(118)

We use the elementary scalar inequality

\displaystyle u\log\left(\frac{u}{v}\right)-u+v\geq\frac{(u-v)^{2}}{2(u+v)},\qquad u,v\geq 0,

(119)

with the usual convention
0\log 0=0
.
Summing (
119
) over
i
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(\widehat{r}_{i}-\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star})^{2}}{\widehat{r}_{i}+\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}}\leq 2{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star}).

(120)

Since
0\leq\widehat{b}_{i}\leq C_{\Omega}
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
120
) imply

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} X_{n}^{\star}-Y_{n}&=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}-\widehat{r}_{i})\widehat{b}_{i}\\
&\leq\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{(\widehat{r}_{i}-\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star})^{2}}{\widehat{r}_{i}+\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{r}_{i}+\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star})\widehat{b}_{i}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\sqrt{2{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})}\left(C_{\Omega}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\widehat{r}_{i}+\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star})\widehat{b}_{i}\right)^{1/2}\\
&=\sqrt{2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})}\sqrt{X_{n}^{\star}+Y_{n}}.\end{aligned}

(121)

Using
ab\leq{1/2}a^{2}+{1/2}b^{2}
with
a=\sqrt{2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})}
and
b=\sqrt{X_{n}^{\star}+Y_{n}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle X_{n}^{\star}-Y_{n}\leq C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})+\tfrac{1}{2}(X_{n}^{\star}+Y_{n}).

(122)

Rearranging yields
X_{n}^{\star}\leq 3Y_{n}+2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})
, which is the claim.

∎

Theorem 20

(Map risk of
\widehat{T}^{1\mathrm{NN}}
)
.

Assume that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
, and
4
hold.
By Assumption
1
, the map
T_{0}(x)=x-\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)
is Lipschitz with constant
L_{T}\coloneqq\kappa^{-1}
.
Let

\displaystyle w(x)\coloneqq e^{-\varphi_{0}(x)},\qquad w_{-}\coloneqq\inf_{x\in\Omega}w(x),\qquad w_{+}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in\Omega}w(x),

(123)

and define the mass-accuracy event

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n,m}\coloneqq\left\{|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|\leq\frac{M_{\mu}}{2},\ |\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|\leq\frac{M_{\nu}}{2}\right\}.

(124)

Then, on
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
, almost surely,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}(x)&\leq 4\frac{w_{+}}{w_{-}}nM_{n}\left(3\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}+2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})\right)\\
&\quad+2L_{T}^{2}\gamma_{0}(\Omega)R_{n}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(125)

Consequently, if

\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|]<\infty
,
then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on

d,\beta_{\min},\beta_{\max},\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0},M_{\mu},M_{\nu},\|\varphi_{0}\|_{\infty},\|\zeta_{0}\|_{\infty},\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty},L_{T},\Omega
,
such that for all sufficiently large
n,m
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}(x)\Bigr]&\leq C\log n\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]+a_{n}+b_{m}\Bigr)\\
&\quad+C\left({(\log n)/n}\right)^{2/d}+C{(\log n)/n},\end{aligned}

(126)

where
a_{n},b_{m}
are the rates from Assumption
4
.

Proof of Theorem
20
.

Let

\displaystyle\widehat{b}_{i}\coloneqq\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2}.

(127)

For
x\in V_{i}
, one has
\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)=\widehat{T}_{i}
, hence

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq 2\widehat{b}_{i}+2\|T_{0}(X_{i})-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq 2\widehat{b}_{i}+2L_{T}^{2}\|X_{i}-x\|^{2}.

(128)

Integrating over
V_{i}
with respect to
\gamma_{0}
and summing over
i
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}(x)\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\gamma_{0}(V_{i})\widehat{b}_{i}+2L_{T}^{2}\gamma_{0}(\Omega)R_{n}^{2}.

(129)

On
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
, we have
\widehat{M}_{\mu}\geq M_{\mu}/2
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}(V_{i})=\int_{V_{i}}wd\mu\leq w_{+}\mu(V_{i})=M_{\mu}w_{+}\bar{\mu}(V_{i})\leq M_{\mu}w_{+}M_{n},

(130)

whereas

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}=w(X_{i}){\widehat{M}_{\mu}/n}\geq w_{-}\frac{M_{\mu}}{2n}.

(131)

Hence, on
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}(V_{i})\leq 2\frac{w_{+}}{w_{-}}nM_{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}.

(132)

Substituting this into (
129
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}(x)\leq 4\frac{w_{+}}{w_{-}}nM_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\widehat{b}_{i}+2L_{T}^{2}\gamma_{0}(\Omega)R_{n}^{2}.

(133)

Applying Lemma
19
proves
(
125
).

For the expectation bound, note first that
\widehat{T}_{i}\in\Omega
for every
i
:
if
\widehat{r}_{i}=0
, then
\widehat{T}_{i}=X_{i}\in\Omega
, while if
\widehat{r}_{i}>0
, then
\widehat{T}_{i}
is a convex combination of
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{m}\in\Omega
and
\Omega
is convex.
Since also
T_{0}(x)\in\Omega
for every
x\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}(x)\leq C_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\Omega)

(134)

almost surely.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0}\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}\Bigr]\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0}\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}\Bigr]+C_{\Omega}\gamma_{0}(\Omega)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n,m}^{c}).

(135)

By Markov’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{n,m}^{c})\leq\frac{2}{M_{\mu}}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|]+\frac{2}{M_{\nu}}\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|].

(136)

Set

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{n}\coloneqq 3\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}+2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star}).

(137)

Then (
125
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0}\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}\leq 4\frac{w_{+}}{w_{-}}nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}+2L_{T}^{2}\gamma_{0}(\Omega)R_{n}^{2}.

(138)

By Lemma
18
, there exists
c>0
such that

\displaystyle m_{n}\coloneqq c{(\log n)/n}

(139)

satisfies
\mathbb{P}(M_{n}\geq m_{n})\leq n^{-2}
for all large
n
.
Hence

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}[nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}]&=\mathbb{E}[nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\mathbf{1}\{M_{n}<m_{n}\}]\\
&\quad+\mathbb{E}[nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\mathbf{1}\{M_{n}\geq m_{n}\}].\end{aligned}

(140)

The first term is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\mathbf{1}\{M_{n}<m_{n}\}]\leq c\log n\mathbb{E}[\widehat{Z}_{n}].

(141)

We next show that
\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\leq C_{0}\log n
for a deterministic constant
C_{0}
.
Let

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{n}^{row}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}.

(142)

On
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
, the zero plan is feasible for
\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})
and therefore

\displaystyle\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})\leq(\widehat{M}_{\mu}+\widehat{M}_{\nu})\leq\frac{3}{2}(M_{\mu}+M_{\nu}).

(143)

Since the transport term and the column
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
term are nonnegative,

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{\mu})\leq\frac{3}{2}(M_{\mu}+M_{\nu}).

(144)

By Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{\mu})\geq\widehat{M}_{n}^{row}\log\left(\frac{\widehat{M}_{n}^{row}}{\widehat{M}_{\mu}}\right)-\widehat{M}_{n}^{row}+\widehat{M}_{\mu}.

(145)

Because
\widehat{M}_{\mu}\in[M_{\mu}/2,3M_{\mu}/2]
on
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
, there exists a deterministic constant
M_{\star}>0
, depending only on
M_{\mu}
and
M_{\nu}
, such that

\displaystyle\widehat{M}_{n}^{row}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\leq M_{\star}.

(146)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\leq C_{\Omega}M_{\star}.

(147)

Also, on
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}=w(X_{i}){\widehat{M}_{\mu}/n}\geq\frac{w_{-}M_{\mu}}{2n},\qquad\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\leq\frac{3}{2}M_{\mu}w_{+}.

(148)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})&=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\widehat{r}_{i}\log\left(\frac{\widehat{r}_{i}}{\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}}\right)-\widehat{r}_{i}+\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\right]\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}\log\left(\frac{\widehat{M}_{n}^{row}}{w_{-}M_{\mu}/(2n)}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\\
&\leq M_{\star}\log\left(\frac{2M_{\star}n}{w_{-}M_{\mu}}\right)+\frac{3}{2}M_{\mu}w_{+}.\end{aligned}

(149)

Combining (
147
) and
(
149
), we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\leq C_{0}\log n

(150)

for all large
n
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}\mathbf{1}\{M_{n}\geq m_{n}\}]\leq n\mathbb{P}(M_{n}\geq m_{n})C_{0}\log n\leq C{(\log n)/n}.

(151)

Combining (
140
),
(
141
), and
(
151
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[nM_{n}\widehat{Z}_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{A}_{n,m}}]\leq C\log n\mathbb{E}[\widehat{Z}_{n}]+C{(\log n)/n}.

(152)

Next, Proposition
17
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}]\leq C\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]+a_{n}+b_{m}\Bigr)

(153)

for a constant
C>0
depending only on the model parameters, where
a_{n},b_{m}
are the rates from Assumption
4
.

Lemma
24
below provides the matching bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})]\leq C\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]+a_{n}+b_{m}\Bigr)

(154)

for another constant
C>0
.
Substituting (
153
) and
(
154
) into
(
152
), then combining
(
135
),
(
136
),
(
138
),
(
152
), and
Lemma
18
(ii), proves
(
126
).
∎

E.3.
Active-source and growth estimation for the 1NN extension

The row marginals of a fitted UOT plan directly encode the active-source ratio

w_{0}=a_{0}^{2}=e^{-\varphi_{0}}
. They do not directly encode the Gaussian–Hellinger growth factor

\lambda_{0}=w_{0}\exp(\|x-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}/4)
.
Hence the proof separates two steps: first we estimate the active-source factor
a_{0}
from the row marginals, and then we transfer the active-factor and map errors to the corrected
\lambda_{0}
-risk.

Lemma 21

(Square-root lower bound on
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
)
.

For every
a,b\geq 0
, with the conventions
0\log 0=0
and
a\log(a/0)=+\infty
for
a>0
, we have

\displaystyle a\log\left(\frac{a}{b}\right)-a+b\geq\left(\sqrt{a}-\sqrt{b}\right)^{2}.

(155)

Proof of Lemma
21
.

If
b=0
, then (
155
) is immediate from the stated conventions, so we may assume that
b>0
.
Writing
a=tb
, it is enough to prove that

\displaystyle t\log t-t+1\geq(\sqrt{t}-1)^{2},\qquad t\geq 0.

Set
s=\sqrt{t}
. Then

\displaystyle t\log t-t+1-(\sqrt{t}-1)^{2}=2s^{2}\log s-2s^{2}+2s=2s(s\log s-s+1)\geq 0,

because
u\mapsto u\log u-u+1
is nonnegative on
[0,\infty)
.
∎

Lemma 22

(Pointwise bound on
|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}
)
.

Let
x\in\Omega
, let
T,T^{0}\in\Omega
, and let
w^{0}\in[w_{-},w_{+}]
. Define

\displaystyle a^{0}=\sqrt{w^{0}},\qquad\lambda^{0}=w^{0}\exp\left(\frac{1}{4}\|x-T^{0}\|^{2}\right).

For any
u\geq 0
, set

\displaystyle\bar{w}=\clip_{[w_{-},w_{+}]}(u),\qquad\bar{\lambda}=\bar{w}\exp\left(\frac{1}{4}\|x-T\|^{2}\right).

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
w_{-},w_{+}
and
\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)
, such that

\displaystyle|\bar{\lambda}-\lambda^{0}|^{2}\leq C\left(|\sqrt{u}-a^{0}|^{2}+\|T-T^{0}\|^{2}\right).

(156)

Proof of Lemma
22
.

Since
w^{0}\in[w_{-},w_{+}]
, clipping can only move
u
closer to
w^{0}
; hence

\displaystyle|\sqrt{\bar{w}}-\sqrt{w^{0}}|\leq|\sqrt{u}-\sqrt{w^{0}}|.

Moreover,
|\bar{w}-w^{0}|\leq 2\sqrt{w_{+}}|\sqrt{\bar{w}}-\sqrt{w^{0}}|
. On the bounded domain, the map
T\mapsto\exp(\|x-T\|^{2}/4)
is uniformly bounded and uniformly Lipschitz for
x,T\in\Omega
. Therefore

\displaystyle|\bar{\lambda}-\lambda^{0}|

\displaystyle\leq C|\bar{w}-w^{0}|+C\|T-T^{0}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(|\sqrt{u}-a^{0}|+\|T-T^{0}\|\right),

and squaring proves the claim.
∎

Lemma 23

(Bound on
\int|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0}|^{2}d\widehat{\eta}
)
.

Let
\widehat{\eta}
be a finite positive measure on a bounded set
\Omega
, let
\widehat{a},a_{0}:\Omega\to[0,\infty)
, and let
\widehat{T},T_{0}:\Omega\to\Omega
be measurable. Suppose
a_{0}^{2}=w_{0}\in[w_{-},w_{+}]
and set
\widehat{\gamma}_{0}=\widehat{a}^{2}\widehat{\eta}
. Define

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}(x)\coloneqq\clip_{[w_{-},w_{+}]}\bigl(\widehat{a}(x)^{2}\bigr)\exp\left(\frac{1}{4}\|x-\widehat{T}(x)\|^{2}\right),\qquad\lambda_{0}(x)\coloneqq w_{0}(x)\exp\left(\frac{1}{4}\|x-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\right).

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
w_{-},w_{+}
and
\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\eta}(x)\leq C\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{a}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\eta}(x)+C\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0}(x).

(157)

Proof of Lemma
23
.

The pointwise argument in Lemma
22
gives

\displaystyle|\widehat{\lambda}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}\leq C|\widehat{a}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}+C\|\widehat{T}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}.

To integrate the last term with respect to
\widehat{\gamma}_{0}
instead of
\widehat{\eta}
, we
prove the pointwise inequality

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq C^{\prime}\widehat{a}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}+C^{\prime}|\widehat{a}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2},\qquad x\in\Omega,

(158)

for
C^{\prime}\coloneqq\max\left(\tfrac{4}{w_{-}},\tfrac{4\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{2}}{w_{-}}\right)
.
Fix
x\in\Omega
and split into two cases.

Case 1:
\widehat{a}(x)^{2}\geq w_{-}/4
.
Then

\widehat{a}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}-T_{0}\|^{2}\geq(w_{-}/4)\|\widehat{T}-T_{0}\|^{2}
, hence

\|\widehat{T}-T_{0}\|^{2}\leq(4/w_{-})\widehat{a}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}-T_{0}\|^{2}
.

Case 2:
\widehat{a}(x)^{2}<w_{-}/4
.
Since
a_{0}^{2}(x)=w_{0}(x)\geq w_{-}
,

a_{0}(x)\geq\sqrt{w_{-}}
, while
\widehat{a}(x)<\sqrt{w_{-}}/2
, so

|\widehat{a}(x)-a_{0}(x)|\geq a_{0}(x)-\widehat{a}(x)\geq\sqrt{w_{-}}/2
, i.e.
|\widehat{a}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}\geq w_{-}/4
. Combined with the trivial bound

\|\widehat{T}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{2}
, we obtain

\|\widehat{T}-T_{0}\|^{2}\leq(4\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{2}/w_{-})|\widehat{a}-a_{0}|^{2}
.

In either case (
158
) holds.
Multiplying by
\widehat{\eta}
and integrating, and noting that

\widehat{a}(x)^{2}d\widehat{\eta}(x)=d\widehat{\gamma}_{0}(x)
by definition of
\widehat{\gamma}_{0}
,
yields (
157
).
∎

Lemma 24

(Bound on
\mathbb{E}[D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})]
)
.

Assume that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
, and
4
hold.
Let
\widehat{\gamma}
be any optimizer of
\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})
, define

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{i}\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\widehat{\gamma}_{ij},\qquad\widehat{\mu}_{i}\coloneqq{\widehat{M}_{\mu}/n},\qquad\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\coloneqq e^{-\varphi_{0}(X_{i})}\widehat{\mu}_{i},

and write
\widehat{r}=(\widehat{r}_{i})_{i=1}^{n}
and
\widehat{r}^{\star}=(\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star})_{i=1}^{n}
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on

M_{\mu},M_{\nu},\|\zeta_{0}\|_{\infty},\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}

and
C_{\Lambda}
from Proposition
7
, such that for all sufficiently large
n,m
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})]\leq C\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]+a_{n}+b_{m}\Bigr),

(159)

where
a_{n},b_{m}
are the rates from Assumption
4
.

Proof of Lemma
24
.

By (
105
) and the nonnegativity of the transport-slack term and of
{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{s}\mid\widehat{s}^{\star})
, the inequality

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})\leq\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}

holds.
By the stability bound (
56
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}-\mu)+\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}-\nu)+C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})+M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\bigl(|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|\bigr)+e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}\bigl(|\log\alpha_{n}|+|\log\beta_{m}|\bigr),

using
M_{\mu}|\alpha_{n}-1|=|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|
and
M_{\nu}|\beta_{m}-1|=|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|
.
Subtracting

\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\mu)+\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\nu)

from both sides and using

\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)=\int\zeta_{0}d\mu+\int\xi_{0}d\nu

gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n},\widehat{\nu}_{m})-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})+M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigl(|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}\bigl(|\log\alpha_{n}|+|\log\beta_{m}|\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}-\widetilde{\mu}_{n})-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}-\widetilde{\nu}_{m}).

The last two integrals are bounded in absolute value by

\displaystyle\|\zeta_{0}\|_{\infty}|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|.

Taking expectations of the resulting inequality, the unbiased linear terms vanish:

\mathbb{E}[\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}-\mu)]=\mathbb{E}[\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}-\nu)]=0
.
By Assumption
4
,
\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|]\leq a_{n}
and the a.s. lower bound
\widehat{M}_{\mu}\geq cM_{\mu}
gives
|\log\alpha_{n}|\leq|\alpha_{n}-1|/c
a.s., so
M_{\mu}\mathbb{E}[|\log\alpha_{n}|]\leq a_{n}/c
. Hence

e\sqrt{M_{\mu}M_{\nu}}\mathbb{E}[|\log\alpha_{n}|]\leq c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\nu}/M_{\mu}}a_{n}.

The analogous bounds hold on the
\nu
-side with
b_{m}
.
Setting

C\coloneqq C_{\Lambda}+\|\zeta_{0}\|_{\infty}+\|\xi_{0}\|_{\infty}+1+c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\nu}/M_{\mu}}+c^{-1}e\sqrt{M_{\mu}/M_{\nu}}

and combining the displays proves (
159
).
∎

Lemma 25

(Bound on
\sum_{i}\widehat{\mu}_{i}|\widehat{a}_{i}-a_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}
)
.

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}|\widehat{a}_{i}-a_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}\leq{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star}).

(160)

Consequently, with
a_{n},b_{m}
as in Assumption
4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}|\widehat{a}_{i}-a_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}\right]\leq C\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]+a_{n}+b_{m}\Bigr).

(161)

Proof of Lemma
25
.

Since
\widehat{r}_{i}=\widehat{\mu}_{i}\widehat{a}_{i}^{2}
and
\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}=\widehat{\mu}_{i}a_{0}(X_{i})^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\left[\widehat{a}_{i}^{2}\log\left(\frac{\widehat{a}_{i}^{2}}{a_{0}(X_{i})^{2}}\right)-\widehat{a}_{i}^{2}+a_{0}(X_{i})^{2}\right].

Lemma
21
gives (
160
) termwise, and the expectation bound follows from Lemma
24
.
∎

Lemma 26

(Bound on
\int|\widehat{\lambda}^{1\mathrm{NN}}-\lambda_{0}|^{2}d\mu
)
.

Assume Assumptions
1
,
2
, and
3
. Let
V_{i},M_{n},R_{n}
be as in Lemma
18
. On the mass-accuracy event
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
from (
125
), there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)\leq CnM_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}+CR_{n}^{2}.

(162)

Proof of Lemma
26
.

Assumption
1
, compactness of
\Omega
, and the compactness of
\Omega
imply that
\lambda_{0}=w_{0}\exp(\|x-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}/4)
is Lipschitz; write
L_{\lambda}=\mathrm{Lip}(\lambda_{0})
. For
x\in V_{i}
,

|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}\leq 2|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}+2L_{\lambda}^{2}\|x-X_{i}\|^{2}.

Integrating over
V_{i}
and using
\mu(V_{i})=M_{\mu}\bar{\mu}(V_{i})\leq M_{\mu}M_{n}
and, on
\mathcal{A}_{n,m}
,
\widehat{\mu}_{i}=\widehat{M}_{\mu}/n\geq M_{\mu}/(2n)
, gives

\mu(V_{i})\leq 2nM_{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}.

Summing over
i
gives (
162
).
∎

Theorem 27

(Growth risk of
\widehat{\lambda}^{1\mathrm{NN}}
)
.

Assume that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
, and
4
hold.
Let
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}
and
\widehat{\lambda}^{\mathrm{1NN}}
be defined by (
10
) and (
11
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all sufficiently large
n,m
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\Bigl[\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}^{\mathrm{1NN}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}(x)\Bigr]&\leq C\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]+\mathbb{E}[M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]+a_{n}+b_{m}\Bigr),\end{aligned}

(163)

where
a_{n},b_{m}
are the rates from Assumption
4
.

Proof of Theorem
27
.

We first prove an in-sample bound and then pass to population loss through Lemma
26
.
Apply Lemma
22
with

\displaystyle u_{i}=\widehat{r}_{i}/\widehat{\mu}_{i},\qquad w_{i}^{0}=w_{0}(X_{i})=e^{-\varphi_{0}(X_{i})},\qquad T_{i}=\widehat{T}_{i},\qquad T_{i}^{0}=T_{0}(X_{i}).

This yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}|\widehat{a}_{i}-a_{0}(X_{i})|^{2}+C\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2}.

(164)

The first term is controlled by Lemma
25
. For the second term, since
w_{0}\geq w_{-}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\mu}_{i}\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2}\leq w_{-}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2}.

Lemma
19
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{r}_{i}^{\star}\|\widehat{T}_{i}-T_{0}(X_{i})\|^{2}\leq 3\widehat{\Delta}_{nm}^{\mathrm{bar}}+2C_{\Omega}{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{r}\mid\widehat{r}^{\star}).

Taking expectations and using Proposition
17
together with Lemma
24
proves (
163
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

The map bound in (
20
) follows from Theorem
20
. The growth bound in the same display is the bound for the correct Gaussian–Hellinger factor
\lambda_{0}
and follows from Theorem
27
. It remains only to substitute explicit rates for the weighted empirical measures.

By Assumptions
2
and
3
, the normalized measures
\bar{\mu}
and
\bar{\nu}
are supported on a bounded subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and have finite moments of every order. Choose any
q>4
. Theorem 1 of
[
16
]
, applied with
p=2
, yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\mu}_{n},\bar{\mu})]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{emp}}(d),\qquad\mathbb{E}[W_{2}^{2}(\bar{\nu}_{m},\bar{\nu})]\leq C_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{emp}}(d).

Substituting these bounds together with the mass-estimation bounds from Assumption
4
into Theorems
20
and
27
yields (
20
). The additional logarithm in the theorem statement covers the nearest-neighbor population extension term in the map bound.

Additionally, since
d\gamma_{0}=w_{0}d\mu
with

w_{0}=e^{-\varphi_{0}}
and, by Assumption
1
,

w_{0}\geq w_{-}>0
on
\Omega
, we have

\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0}\|^{2}d\mu=\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0}\|^{2}w_{0}^{-1}d\gamma_{0}\leq w_{-}^{-1}\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}^{\mathrm{1NN}}-T_{0}\|^{2}d\gamma_{0}.

Taking expectations and absorbing
w_{-}^{-1}
into the constant gives the desired

d\mu
-risk bound.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Theorem
4

Here, we additionally define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}).

(165)

After positive-part renormalization, let

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x))_{+}}{\int_{[0,1]^{d}}(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(u))_{+}du},\qquad\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(y)\coloneqq\frac{(\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(y))_{+}}{\int_{[0,1]^{d}}(\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(v))_{+}dv}.

(166)

We then define the oracle equal-mass measures

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\coloneqq M_{\mu}\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)dx,\qquad\widetilde{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\cdot)\coloneqq M_{\nu}\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(y)dy,

(167)

We also define a useful rate

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha)\coloneqq n^{{-(\alpha-1)/(2(\alpha-1)+d)}}.

(168)

F.1.
Convergence rates of boundary-adapted kernel density estimator

For a zero-mean
r\in L^{2}([0,1]^{d})
, we write

\displaystyle\|r\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}\coloneqq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{|\langle r,\eta_{k}\rangle_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d})}|^{2}}{\lambda_{k}}.

(169)

This is the natural negative-order Sobolev norm associated with the Neumann Laplacian eigenbasis.

Lemma 28

(Neumann
H^{-1}
controls
W_{2}
on the cube)
.

Let
f
and
g
be probability densities on
[0,1]^{d}
satisfying

\displaystyle 0<\underline{\beta}\leq f(x),g(x)\leq\overline{\beta}<\infty,\qquad x\in[0,1]^{d}.

(170)

Then

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(fdx,gdx)\leq\underline{\beta}^{-1}\|f-g\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}.

(171)

Proof of Lemma
28
.

Set
r\coloneqq g-f
.
Because
\int_{[0,1]^{d}}r=0
, there exists a unique weak solution
u
of the Neumann problem

\displaystyle-\Delta u=r,\qquad\partial_{\nu}u=0\text{ on }\partial[0,1]^{d},\qquad\int_{[0,1]^{d}}u=0.

Writing
u=\sum_{k\neq 0}u_{k}\eta_{k}
gives
u_{k}=\langle r,\eta_{k}\rangle/\lambda_{k}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\nabla u(x)\|^{2}dx=\sum_{k\neq 0}\lambda_{k}|u_{k}|^{2}=\sum_{k\neq 0}\frac{|\langle r,\eta_{k}\rangle|^{2}}{\lambda_{k}}=\|r\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}.

Now set
f_{t}=(1-t)f+tg
for
t\in[0,1]
and define

\displaystyle v_{t}(x)\coloneqq\frac{\nabla u(x)}{f_{t}(x)}.

Since
f_{t}\geq\underline{\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f_{t}+\nabla\cdot(f_{t}v_{t})=(g-f)+\nabla\cdot(\nabla u)=0,

so
(f_{t},v_{t})
is an admissible Benamou-Brenier path from
fdx
to
gdx
.
Hence

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(fdx,gdx)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}f_{t}(x)\|v_{t}(x)\|^{2}dxdt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\frac{\|\nabla u(x)\|^{2}}{f_{t}(x)}dxdt

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\beta}^{-1}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\nabla u(x)\|^{2}dx=\underline{\beta}^{-1}\|f-g\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2},

which proves (
171
).
∎

Proposition 29

(Cube kernel density rates)
.

Set
s\coloneqq\alpha-1>0
.
Assume that
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, that Assumptions
5
hold, and that

\displaystyle\beta_{\min}\leq p(x),q(x)\leq\beta_{\max}\quad\text{for all }x\in[0,1]^{d}.

Let

\displaystyle L_{n}\asymp n^{1/(d+2s)},\qquad L_{m}\asymp m^{1/(d+2s)}.

(172)

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
d,\alpha,M,\beta_{\min},\beta_{\max}
and the cutoff
\tau
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\mu)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq CM_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\alpha),

(173)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}},\nu)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq CM_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\alpha),

(174)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha),

(175)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}-q\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{L}_{m}(\alpha).

(176)

Proof of Proposition
29
.

We prove the source bounds; the target bounds are identical. For brevity write

\displaystyle m_{n}(k)\coloneqq m_{L_{n}}^{\otimes}(k)=\prod_{r=1}^{d}\tau\left(\frac{\pi^{2}k_{r}^{2}}{L_{n}^{2}}\right),\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}.

Let

\displaystyle\theta_{k}\coloneqq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}p(x)\eta_{k}(x)dx,\qquad\widehat{\theta}_{k}\coloneqq{1/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{k}(X_{i}),

so that
\theta_{0}=1
and

\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}m_{n}(k)\widehat{\theta}_{k}\eta_{k}(x).

(177)

Write

\displaystyle p_{L_{n}}(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\bigl[\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\bigr]=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}m_{n}(k)\theta_{k}\eta_{k}(x).

Equivalently,
p_{L_{n}}
is the restriction to
[0,1]^{d}
of the tensor-product smooth cosine cutoff on the doubled torus with coordinatewise multiplier
m_{n}
.
By Definition
1
, the even reflection
\mathcal{E}p
belongs to
C^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})
with
\|\mathcal{E}p\|_{C^{s}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})}\leq M
.
Let
P_{N}
denote the sharp Fourier projection onto frequencies
\|k\|_{\infty}\leq N
on
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
.
Since
\tau\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,\infty))
with
\tau\equiv 1
on a neighborhood of
0
, there exists
c>0
such that
m_{n}(k)=1
whenever
\|k\|_{\infty}\leq cL_{n}
, and
|1-m_{n}(k)|\leq\mathbf{1}\{\|k\|_{\infty}>cL_{n}\}
for all
k
.
Consequently, by Parseval’s identity on
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|p_{L_{n}}-p\|_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\|\mathcal{E}p-P_{cL_{n}}\mathcal{E}p\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})}.

Applying the multivariate Fourier truncation estimate
[
8
, Eq. (5.8.4)]
to
\mathcal{E}p
on
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
gives

\displaystyle\|p_{L_{n}}-p\|_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d})}\leq CL_{n}^{-s}.

(178)

Moreover, if the
k
th coefficient of
p_{L_{n}}-p
is nonzero, then
m_{n}(k)\neq 1
, so for some coordinate
r
one has
\pi^{2}k_{r}^{2}/L_{n}^{2}\geq 1
and therefore
\lambda_{k}\geq\pi^{2}k_{r}^{2}\geq L_{n}^{2}
.
Hence, by (
169
),

\displaystyle\|p_{L_{n}}-p\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\neq 0}\frac{|1-m_{n}(k)|^{2}|\theta_{k}|^{2}}{\lambda_{k}}

(179)

\displaystyle\leq CL_{n}^{-2}\sum_{k\neq 0}|1-m_{n}(k)|^{2}|\theta_{k}|^{2}

(180)

\displaystyle=CL_{n}^{-2}\|p_{L_{n}}-p\|_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}\leq CL_{n}^{-2(s+1)}=CL_{n}^{-2\alpha},

(181)

where the last inequality uses (
178
).

For the stochastic term, orthonormality of
(\eta_{k})
and the upper bound on
p
imply

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p_{L_{n}}\|_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}\bigr]&=\sum_{k\neq 0}m_{n}(k)^{2}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\theta}_{k})\\
&\leq\frac{C}{n}\#\Bigl\{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}:m_{n}(k)\neq 0\Bigr\}\\
&\leq C\frac{L_{n}^{d}}{n}.\end{aligned}

(182)

Likewise,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p_{L_{n}}\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}\bigr]&=\sum_{k\neq 0}\frac{m_{n}(k)^{2}}{\lambda_{k}}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\theta}_{k})\\
&\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\\
0\leq k_{r}\lesssim L_{n}\end{subarray}}|k|^{-2}\\
&\leq C\begin{cases}n^{-1},&d=1,\\
(\log L_{n})n^{-1},&d=2,\\
L_{n}^{d-2}n^{-1},&d\geq 3.\end{cases}\end{aligned}

(183)

Combining (
178
), (
179
), (
182
), and (
183
), and using Cauchy-Schwarz together with Jensen’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(L_{n}^{-s}+\sqrt{\frac{L_{n}^{d}}{n}}\right),

(184)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C\left(L_{n}^{-2\alpha}+\begin{cases}n^{-1},&d=1,\\
(\log L_{n})n^{-1},&d=2,\\
L_{n}^{d-2}n^{-1},&d\geq 3\end{cases}\right).

(185)

We now derive the corresponding
L^{1}
bound for the renormalized estimator
\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
.
Since
\tau(0)=1
and
\eta_{0}\equiv 1
on
[0,1]^{d}
, the spectral form (
177
) together with
\widehat{\theta}_{0}={1/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{0}(X_{i})=1
gives

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)dx=m_{n}(0)\widehat{\theta}_{0}=1.

Set
u\coloneqq(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})_{+}
and
Z\coloneqq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}u
. Since
\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}=1
, we have

\displaystyle Z-1=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})_{-}.

Moreover, on the set
\{\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}<0\}
we have
p-\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\geq-\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}=(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})_{-}
, hence

\displaystyle Z-1=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})_{-}\leq\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}.

Also,

\displaystyle\|u-\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})_{-}=Z-1,

so by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|u-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\|u-\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}+\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\leq 2\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}.

Finally,

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{u}{Z}-u\right\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}=|1-Z|=Z-1\leq\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})},

which together with the previous display implies

\displaystyle\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\leq 3\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}.

(186)

Taking expectations and combining with the bound on
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}]
above yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]\leq C\left(L_{n}^{-s}+\sqrt{\frac{L_{n}^{d}}{n}}\right).

(187)

It remains to prove the Wasserstein bound.
Let
\underline{p}\coloneqq\inf_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}p(x)>0
.
Since
\tau\in C_{c}^{\infty}([0,\infty))
with
\tau(0)=1
, the convolution kernel of the multiplier operator
T_{m_{n}}\coloneqq f\mapsto\sum_{k}m_{n}(k)\widehat{f}(k)e^{i\pi k\cdot(\cdot)}
on
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
is the periodization of
L_{n}^{d}\psi^{\otimes d}(L_{n}\cdot)
for a fixed Schwartz function
\psi
independent of
n
, and therefore has uniformly bounded
L^{1}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})
norm; by Young’s convolution inequality,
T_{m_{n}}
is uniformly bounded on
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})
. Moreover,
T_{m_{n}}
acts as the identity on cosine polynomials of degree at most
cL_{n}
. Since
\mathcal{E}p
is continuous on
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
, the Weierstrass approximation theorem produces a cosine polynomial
\phi_{n}
of degree
\leq cL_{n}
with
\|\mathcal{E}p-\phi_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})}\to 0
, and writing
\mathcal{E}p-T_{m_{n}}\mathcal{E}p=(\mathcal{E}p-\phi_{n})-T_{m_{n}}(\mathcal{E}p-\phi_{n})
gives

\displaystyle\|p_{L_{n}}-p\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}=\|\mathcal{E}p-T_{m_{n}}\mathcal{E}p\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})}\to 0\qquad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Hence
\inf p_{L_{n}}\geq\underline{p}/2
for all sufficiently large
n
.
Moreover, the product form (
13
) implies

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}K_{L_{n}}(x,x)=\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\prod_{r=1}^{d}\kappa_{L_{n}}(x_{r},x_{r})\leq CL_{n}^{d}.

Define

\displaystyle Pf\coloneqq\int f(z)p(z)dz,\qquad P_{n}f\coloneqq{1/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{i}),\qquad f_{x}(z)\coloneqq K_{L_{n}}(x,z).

Then

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p_{L_{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}=\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}|(P_{n}-P)f_{x}|.

For every
x,z\in[0,1]^{d}
, Cauchy-Schwarz in the cosine expansion gives

\displaystyle|f_{x}(z)|=|K_{L_{n}}(x,z)|\leq K_{L_{n}}(x,x)^{1/2}K_{L_{n}}(z,z)^{1/2}\leq CL_{n}^{d},

so the class
\mathcal{F}_{n}\coloneqq\{f_{x}:x\in[0,1]^{d}\}
has envelope
U_{n}\leq CL_{n}^{d}
.
Likewise, using
p\leq\beta_{\max}
, orthonormality of
(\eta_{k})
, and
0\leq m_{n}(k)\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(f_{x}(X_{1}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[f_{x}(X_{1})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\int K_{L_{n}}(x,z)^{2}p(z)dz

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{\max}\int K_{L_{n}}(x,z)^{2}dz

\displaystyle=\beta_{\max}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}}m_{n}(k)^{2}\eta_{k}(x)^{2}\leq\beta_{\max}K_{L_{n}}(x,x)\leq CL_{n}^{d}.

Hence
\sigma_{n}^{2}\coloneqq\sup_{x}\mathrm{Var}(f_{x}(X_{1}))\leq CL_{n}^{d}
.
For the mean supremum, observe that
\mathcal{F}_{n}
is contained in the finite-dimensional subspace
V_{L_{n}}\coloneqq\mathrm{span}\{\eta_{k}:m_{n}(k)\neq 0\}
of
L^{2}([0,1]^{d})
, of dimension at most
CL_{n}^{d}
. By a standard chaining bound for empirical processes indexed by a uniformly bounded subset of a finite-dimensional class with envelope
U_{n}
and variance
\sigma_{n}^{2}

[
19
, Corollary 3.5.8]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{x}|(P_{n}-P)f_{x}|\Big]\leq C\sqrt{\frac{L_{n}^{d}\log n}{n}}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality to
\mathcal{F}_{n}
therefore yields, for every
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p_{L_{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}>C\left(\sqrt{\frac{L_{n}^{d}t}{n}}+\frac{L_{n}^{d}t}{n}\right)\right)\leq 2e^{-t}.

(188)

Taking
t=A\log n
and using
L_{n}^{d}\log n/n\to 0
, we obtain

\Pr\left(\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p_{L_{n}}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}>\frac{\underline{p}}{4}\right)\leq C_{A}n^{-A}

(189)

for every fixed
A>0
and all sufficiently large
n
.
Therefore the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{n}\coloneqq\left\{\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}\leq\frac{\underline{p}}{2}\right\}

satisfies
\Pr(\mathcal{G}_{n}^{c})\leq C_{A}n^{-A}
.
On
\mathcal{G}_{n}
we have
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\geq\underline{p}/2>0
, so the positive-part renormalization is inactive and

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}=\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

Since
p\geq\underline{p}
and
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\geq\underline{p}/2
on
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, Lemma
28
gives

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}\bigl(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}dx,pdx\bigr)=W_{2}^{2}\bigl(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}dx,pdx\bigr)\leq\frac{2}{\underline{p}}\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{H^{-1}_{N}([0,1]^{d})}^{2}\qquad\text{on }\mathcal{G}_{n}.

On the complement, both measures are supported in
[0,1]^{d}
, so

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}\bigl(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}dx,pdx\bigr)\leq d.

Taking expectations and using (
185
) therefore yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}dx,pdx)\bigr]\leq C\left(L_{n}^{-2\alpha}+\begin{cases}n^{-1},&d=1,\\
(\log L_{n})n^{-1},&d=2,\\
L_{n}^{d-2}n^{-1},&d\geq 3\end{cases}\right).

(190)

Now choose
L_{n}\asymp n^{1/(d+2s)}
.
Then (
187
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]\leq Cn^{{-s/(2s+d)}}=C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha),

while (
190
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}dx,pdx)\bigr]\leq C\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\alpha).

Finally,

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\mu)=M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}dx,pdx),

which proves (
173
) and (
175
).
The target bounds (
174
)-(
176
) are identical.
∎

F.2.
Proof of the upper bound

One key ingredient to the proof is the following result from
[
5
]
that guarantees the Lipschitzness of the optimal transport map on the torus, given that the densities are bounded above and away from zero:

Proposition 30

(Regularity of OT on the doubled torus)
.

Let
P,Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ac}}([0,1]^{d})
admit densities
p,q
satisfying
0<\beta_{\min}\leq p(x),q(x)\leq\beta_{\max}<\infty
. Let
\mathcal{E}P,\mathcal{E}Q\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d})
denote their respective even
2\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-periodic reflections on the doubled torus
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}=(\mathbb{R}/2\mathbb{Z})^{d}
, with densities
\mathcal{E}p,\mathcal{E}q
.

Then, there exists an optimal transport map
T_{\mathcal{E}}=\nabla\Psi
from
\mathcal{E}P
to
\mathcal{E}Q
, where the Brenier potential
\Psi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
is a convex function satisfying the following properties:

(1)

Periodicity
: The map
x\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}-\Psi(x)
is
2\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-periodic, and
T_{\mathcal{E}}(x+2k)=T_{\mathcal{E}}(x)+2k
for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

(2)

Symmetry and Restriction
: Because the densities
\mathcal{E}p
and
\mathcal{E}q
are coordinatewise even, the map
T_{\mathcal{E}}
leaves the hypercube
[0,1]^{d}
invariant. The restriction of
T_{\mathcal{E}}
to
[0,1]^{d}
uniquely determines the optimal transport map
T_{0}
from
P
to
Q
.

(3)

Lipschitz Continuity
: There exists a constant
\lambda\in(0,1)
such that
\Psi
is strongly convex and has a uniformly bounded Hessian:

\displaystyle\lambda I_{d}\preceq\nabla^{2}\Psi(x)\preceq\lambda^{-1}I_{d},\quad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

(191)

Consequently, the restricted transport map
T_{0}=\nabla\Psi|_{[0,1]^{d}}
and its optimal inverse
S_{0}=\nabla\Psi^{*}|_{[0,1]^{d}}
are Lipschitz on the hypercube
[0,1]^{d}
.

We are now in a position to prove the main bounds of the kernel-based estimator.

Corollary 31

(Kernel-based estimator’s plug-in rate)
.

Assume that
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
,
c(x,y)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}
, and that Assumptions
1
,
3
, and
4
hold.
Let

\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\ \widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}},\ \widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}},\ \widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}},\ \widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

Let
a_{n},b_{m}
be the sequences from Assumption
4
.
Suppose that, for some deterministic sequences
r_{n},r_{m},\ell_{n},\ell_{m}\geq 0
, where
r_{n}\geq 1/n
and
r_{m}>1/m
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\mu)\bigr]\leq r_{n},\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}},\nu)\bigr]\leq r_{m},

(192)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]\leq\ell_{n},\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}-q\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})}\bigr]\leq\ell_{m}.

(193)

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the constants in Assumption
1
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(r_{n}+r_{m}+a_{n}+b_{m}),

(194)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C(r_{n}+r_{m}+a_{n}+b_{m}),

(195)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)\right|\right]&\leq M_{\mu}\|\zeta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}\ell_{n}+M_{\nu}\|\xi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}\ell_{m}\\
&\qquad+C(r_{n}+r_{m}+a_{n}+b_{m}).\end{aligned}

(196)

Proof of Corollary
31
.

Let
\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
be an optimal plan for

\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
, with marginals

\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
.

We first derive an algebraic identity that decomposes the empirical UOT cost into a transport-cost term and two
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
penalties against oracle reference measures. By optimality of
\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
for
\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}})
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}=\int\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,y)+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr).

(197)

We rewrite the source
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
term with the new reference measure
\gamma_{0}^{\mathrm{or}}\coloneqq e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
. If
f=d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}/d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
, then
d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}/d\gamma_{0}^{\mathrm{or}}=fe^{\varphi_{0}}
, and therefore

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\gamma_{0}^{\mathrm{or}}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int\Bigl[f\log\bigl(fe^{\varphi_{0}}\bigr)-f+e^{-\varphi_{0}}\Bigr]d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}

\displaystyle={D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)+\int\varphi_{0}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},

where we used
\zeta_{0}=-(e^{-\varphi_{0}}-1)
. Hence

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)={D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\gamma_{0}^{\mathrm{or}}\bigr)-\int\varphi_{0}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}+\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

(198)

Exactly the same computation with
\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{or}}\coloneqq e^{-\psi_{0}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}
yields

\displaystyle{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)={D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid\gamma_{1}^{\mathrm{or}}\bigr)-\int\psi_{0}d\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}+\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

(199)

Substituting (
198
) and (
199
) into (
197
) and using

\displaystyle\int\varphi_{0}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}+\int\psi_{0}d\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}=\int\bigl(\varphi_{0}(x)+\psi_{0}(y)\bigr)d\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,y),

we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}\\
&\qquad=\int_{[0,1]^{d}\times[0,1]^{d}}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}-\varphi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(y)\Bigr)d\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,y)\\
&\qquad\quad+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid e^{-\psi_{0}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr).\end{aligned}

(200)

By the stability bound (
57
), defining the value-functional excess

\displaystyle V_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\coloneqq\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\nu),

(201)

its expectation satisfies

\mathbb{E}\bigl[V_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr]\leq C(r_{n}+r_{m}+a_{n}+b_{m}).

(202)

Applying Lemma
2
to the integrand in (
200
), we obtain the empirical active-marginal bound:

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\leq V_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

(203)

Directly calculating
{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)
and using (
200
) yield the following bound for the active-source factors:

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\leq{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)\leq V_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

(204)

Since
\varphi_{0}
is continuous, it is uniformly bounded on
[0,1]^{d}
, which guarantees the existence of a constant
w_{-}>0
such that
a_{0}(x)^{2}=e^{-\varphi_{0}(x)}\geq w_{-}
, for all
x\in[0,1]^{d}
. Also, since the images of both
\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
T_{0}
are contained in
[0,1]^{d}
, we have

\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq\mathrm{diam}([0,1]^{d})^{2}=d.
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{w_{-}}a_{0}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}.

(205)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{w_{-}}\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

(206)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{w_{-}}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

(207)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}+C_{\mathrm{tr}}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}.

(208)

For some constant
C_{\mathrm{tr}}>0
. Integrating this bound with respect to
d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)
, and noting that
d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)=\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)
by definition, we transfer the map error from the active marginal to the empirical measure:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)&\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\\
&\quad+C_{\mathrm{tr}}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\\
&\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(2/\kappa+1\bigr)V_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}.\end{aligned}

(209)

Using the notations from Proposition
29
, we define the high-probability event
\mathcal{G}_{nm}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{nm}\coloneqq\left\{\inf_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\geq\frac{\underline{p}}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\inf_{y\in[0,1]^{d}}\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(y)\geq\frac{\underline{q}}{2}\right\},

(210)

where
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
is defined in (
177
),
\underline{p}=\inf_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}p(x)
, and
\widetilde{q}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\underline{q}
are defined analogously. Conditional on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}
, we have
\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}=\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}=\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}
, and the densities are uniformly bounded away from zero. Because the estimators are constructed using the Neumann-compatible cosine kernel (
13
),
\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}
coincide exactly with the restrictions of their even-reflected smooth extensions
\mathcal{E}\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\mathcal{E}\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}
on the doubled torus
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
.

Consequently, the empirical active marginals
\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}=e^{-\widehat{\varphi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}=e^{-\widehat{\psi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}
are strictly bounded away from zero and infinity on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}
. We may therefore apply the torus regularity result of Proposition
30
. Let
\widehat{\Psi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
be the optimal Brenier potential for the extended balanced transport problem on
\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}
. By Proposition
30
, there exists a uniform constant
\widehat{\lambda}\in(0,1)
such that the Hessian is uniformly bounded:

\displaystyle\widehat{\lambda}I_{d}\preceq\nabla^{2}\widehat{\Psi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)\preceq\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}I_{d},\quad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{T}_{2}^{d}.

(211)

Because
\mathcal{E}\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\mathcal{E}\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}
are coordinatewise even, the symmetry guarantees that the transport map
\nabla\widehat{\Psi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
leaves the sub-domain
[0,1]^{d}
invariant. By uniqueness, its restriction to the hypercube is precisely our empirical active transport map:

\displaystyle\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)=\nabla\widehat{\Psi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x),\quad\text{for }x\in[0,1]^{d}.

(212)

The uniform Hessian bound directly implies that
\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
is Lipschitz on
[0,1]^{d}
with constant
L_{T}\coloneqq\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}
. By an identical application of Proposition
30
to the population densities
p,q\in\mathcal{C}_{N}^{\alpha-1}([0,1]^{d};M)
, the population map
T_{0}
is also Lipschitz on
[0,1]^{d}
with constant
L_{0}\coloneqq\lambda^{-1}
.

Let
\widehat{\gamma}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
be the optimal transport plan between
\mu
and
\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}
for the squared Euclidean cost. Squaring the triangle inequality and integrating over
\widehat{\gamma}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,x^{\prime})
, we obtain on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)&=\int_{[0,1]^{d}\times[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,x^{\prime})\\
&\leq 3\int\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,x^{\prime})\\
&\quad+3\int\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})-T_{0}(x^{\prime})\|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})\\
&\quad+3\int\|T_{0}(x^{\prime})-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,x^{\prime})\\
&\leq 3L_{T}^{2}W_{2}^{2}(\mu,\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})+3C_{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(2/\kappa+1\bigr)V_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}+3L_{0}^{2}W_{2}^{2}(\mu,\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}).\end{aligned}

(213)

Now we focus on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}
. Since both the empirical map
\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and the population map
T_{0}
take values in the unit hypercube
[0,1]^{d}
, the squared map error is trivially bounded by the squared Euclidean diameter of the domain,
d
. Hence, it follows from the tail bound (
189
) and the union bound that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{c}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq d\Pr(\mathcal{G}^{c}_{nm})

(214)

\displaystyle\leq d\cdot C_{A}(n^{-A}+m^{-A}).

(215)

By choosing
A\geq 2
, the expectation decays as
O(n^{-2}+m^{-2})
, which is absorbed by the density estimation rates in
W^{2}_{2}
, namely
O(r_{n}+r_{m})
.

Taking expectations of (
213
) and substituting (
202
) yields (
194
).

For the growth factor, Lemma
23
provides the bound under the empirical measure:

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})-\lambda_{0}(x^{\prime})|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq C{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}\bigr)

(216)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})-T_{0}(x^{\prime})\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq CV_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}.

(217)

Since
\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
and
\widehat{\varphi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
have bounded derivatives on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}
, the clipped growth factor
\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}
is also globally Lipschitz with some constant
L_{\lambda}
. Reapplying the same Wasserstein coupling
\widehat{\gamma}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x,x^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)&\leq 3L_{\lambda}^{2}W_{2}^{2}(\mu,\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}})+3\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})-\lambda_{0}(x^{\prime})|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(x^{\prime})\\
&\quad+3L_{\lambda_{0}}^{2}W_{2}^{2}(\mu,\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}).\end{aligned}

(218)

Taking expectations and applying (
202
) and (
216
) proves (
195
).

Finally, taking absolute values in the linearized estimated-mass bound yields

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\mu)\right|+\left|\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\nu)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}},\nu)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\Bigl(|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|+|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|\Bigr).

Because

\displaystyle\left|\int\zeta_{0}d(\widetilde{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\mu)\right|

\displaystyle\leq M_{\mu}\|\zeta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}-p\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})},

\displaystyle\left|\int\xi_{0}d(\widetilde{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}-\nu)\right|

\displaystyle\leq M_{\nu}\|\xi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d})}\|\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}-q\|_{L^{1}([0,1]^{d})},

taking expectations proves (
196
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Apply Corollary
31
with

\displaystyle r_{n}=CM_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\alpha),\qquad r_{m}=CM_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}}(\alpha),

and

\displaystyle\ell_{n}=C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha),\qquad\ell_{m}=C\mathfrak{L}_{m}(\alpha),

which are available from Proposition
29
. Assumption
4
supplies the bounds
\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\mu}-M_{\mu}|]\leq a_{n}
and
\mathbb{E}[|\widehat{M}_{\nu}-M_{\nu}|]\leq b_{m}
, so the corollary yields the two bounds collected in (
25
).
∎

Appendix G
Boundary-corrected wavelet estimator on the hypercube

G.1.
Definition

In this section, we replace the periodic wavelet construction by a boundary-corrected wavelet construction on the unit hypercube
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
. This allows the smooth plugin estimator to operate directly on non-periodic data while keeping the same UOT reduction as before.

Definition 3

(Boundary-corrected wavelet basis)
.

Fix an integer
N\geq 2
and a base scale
j_{0}\geq\lceil\log_{2}N\rceil
. Let
\zeta_{0}^{\mathrm{db}},\xi_{0}^{\mathrm{db}}
denote the compactly supported
N
-regular Daubechies scaling and wavelet functions on
\mathbb{R}
. We follow the standard Cohen-Daubechies-Vial boundary correction.

For each level
j\geq j_{0}
, define the
interior
one-dimensional translates

\displaystyle\zeta_{j,k}^{\mathrm{int}}(t)\coloneqq 2^{j/2}\zeta_{0}^{\mathrm{db}}(2^{j}t-k),\qquad\xi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{int}}(t)\coloneqq 2^{j/2}\xi_{0}^{\mathrm{db}}(2^{j}t-k),

for
N\leq k\leq 2^{j}-N-1
. The boundary-corrected construction replaces the first and last
N
translates at each level by
edge functions

\displaystyle\zeta_{j,k}^{L},\ \xi_{j,k}^{L},\qquad\zeta_{j,k}^{R},\ \xi_{j,k}^{R},\qquad k=0,\ldots,N-1,

satisfying

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}(\zeta_{j,k}^{L}),\operatorname{supp}(\xi_{j,k}^{L})

\displaystyle\subset[0,(2N-1)2^{-j}],

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}(\zeta_{j,k}^{R}),\operatorname{supp}(\xi_{j,k}^{R})

\displaystyle\subset[1-(2N-1)2^{-j},1].

We then define the one-dimensional boundary-corrected scaling and wavelet families by

\displaystyle\zeta_{j,k}^{\mathrm{bc},1}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\zeta_{j,k}^{L}(t),&0\leq k\leq N-1,\\
\zeta_{j,k}^{\mathrm{int}}(t),&N\leq k\leq 2^{j}-N-1,\\
\zeta_{j,2^{j}-1-k}^{R}(t),&2^{j}-N\leq k\leq 2^{j}-1,\end{cases}

(219)

\displaystyle\xi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{bc},1}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\xi_{j,k}^{L}(t),&0\leq k\leq N-1,\\
\xi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{int}}(t),&N\leq k\leq 2^{j}-N-1,\\
\xi_{j,2^{j}-1-k}^{R}(t),&2^{j}-N\leq k\leq 2^{j}-1.\end{cases}

(220)

Thus the correction is local: only the
2N
boundary indices are modified, while all interior indices coincide with the usual Daubechies translates.

Now write
K(j)\coloneqq\{0,\ldots,2^{j}-1\}^{d}
. For
k=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})\in K(j_{0})
,
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\Omega
, and
\ell\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, define

\displaystyle\zeta_{j_{0},k}^{\mathrm{bc}}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{r=1}^{d}\zeta_{j_{0},k_{r}}^{\mathrm{bc},1}(x_{r}),

\displaystyle\xi_{j,k,\ell}^{\mathrm{bc}}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{r:\ell_{r}=0}\zeta_{j,k_{r}}^{\mathrm{bc},1}(x_{r})\prod_{r:\ell_{r}=1}\xi_{j,k_{r}}^{\mathrm{bc},1}(x_{r}),\qquad j\geq j_{0}.

Set

\displaystyle\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\zeta_{j_{0},k}^{\mathrm{bc}}:k\in K(j_{0})\right\},

\displaystyle\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\xi_{j,k,\ell}^{\mathrm{bc}}:k\in K(j),\ \ell\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\right\},\qquad j\geq j_{0}.

The resulting family

\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathrm{bc}}\coloneqq\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}\cup\bigcup_{j\geq j_{0}}\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}

is the boundary-corrected tensor-product wavelet basis on
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
. By the Cohen-Daubechies-Vial construction, it is an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(\Omega)
, and the scaling space
\mathrm{span}(\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}})
contains all tensor-product polynomials of coordinatewise degree at most
N-1
; in particular, it contains the constants.

Remark 3
.

At every scale
j
, the basis agrees with the ordinary Daubechies system on the interior cells
k=N,\ldots,2^{j}-N-1
. The only modification is at the first and last
N
cells, where the translates that would otherwise cross the boundary are replaced by edge functions supported inside
[0,(2N-1)2^{-j}]
and
[1-(2N-1)2^{-j},1]
. In particular, the cube estimator does not periodize the data or wrap information across opposite faces of
[0,1]^{d}
; the non-periodic behavior is handled locally at the boundary.

Definition 4

(Boundary-corrected wavelet plugin estimator)
.

Let

\Psi^{\mathrm{bc}}=\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}\cup\bigcup_{j\geq j_{0}}\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}

be the boundary-corrected tensor-product wavelet basis from Definition
3
. For
\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}
and
\xi\in\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}
, define the empirical coefficients

\widehat{\alpha}_{\zeta}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\zeta(X_{i}),\widehat{\beta}_{\zeta}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\zeta(Y_{j}),

and

\widehat{\alpha}_{\xi}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi(X_{i}),\widehat{\beta}_{\xi}\coloneqq\tfrac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\xi(Y_{j}).

Given resolution levels
J_{n},J_{m}\geq j_{0}
, the preliminary boundary-corrected wavelet estimators of the normalized densities
p
and
q
are

\displaystyle\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}\widehat{\alpha}_{\zeta}\zeta(x)+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\sum_{\xi\in\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}}\widehat{\alpha}_{\xi}\xi(x),\quad\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(y)\coloneqq\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}\widehat{\beta}_{\zeta}\zeta(y)+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{m}}\sum_{\xi\in\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}}\widehat{\beta}_{\xi}\xi(y).

(221)

Since these preliminary estimators may take negative values, we define the positive-part renormalizations

\displaystyle\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x))_{+}}{\int_{\Omega}(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(u))_{+}du},\qquad\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(y)\coloneqq\frac{(\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(y))_{+}}{\int_{\Omega}(\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(v))_{+}dv}.

(222)

We then set

\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}\coloneqq M_{\mu}\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx
and

\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}\coloneqq M_{\nu}\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx
.
The
boundary-corrected wavelet plugin UOT estimator
is defined as the fitted transport-growth pair

\displaystyle\bigl(\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\bigr)\coloneqq\bigl(T_{\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}},\lambda_{\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}}\bigr),

(223)

whenever the fitted problem between
\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}
is Monge-type in the above sense.

Remark 4
.

The positive-part renormalization in (
222
) is included to guarantee that the fitted object is a probability density on
\Omega
. Theorem
33
below shows that this truncation does not alter the
L^{1}
rate. If the masses
M_{\mu}
and
M_{\nu}
are unknown, they may again be replaced by external estimators
\widehat{M}_{\mu}
and
\widehat{M}_{\nu}
without changing the definition of the fitted pair.

G.2.
Theoretical Analysis

For the boundary-corrected wavelet construction on the cube, the rate statement becomes completely explicit. Write

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)\coloneqq\begin{cases}n^{-1},&d=1,\\
(\log n)^{2}n^{-1},&d=2,\\
n^{{-2\alpha/(2(\alpha-1)+d)}},&d\geq 3.\end{cases}

(224)

Theorem 32

(Wavelet plugin rate)
.

Assume that
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, that
c(x,y)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}
, and let

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}

be the boundary-corrected wavelet plugin objects from Definition
4
.
Assume Assumptions
1
and
3
for the unit cube, Assumption
2
holds automatically.
Assume moreover that
\alpha>1
and
\alpha\notin\mathbb{N}
, that the densities satisfy

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-1}\leq p(x),q(x)\leq\Gamma,\qquad\|p\|_{C^{\alpha-1}(\Omega)}+\|q\|_{C^{\alpha-1}(\Omega)}\leq M,

(225)

that the boundary-corrected wavelet basis in Definition
3
has regularity strictly greater than
\alpha-1
, that the fitted wavelet problems are Monge-type, and write

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\coloneqq(\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

Assume also that

2^{J_{n}}\asymp n^{{1}/({d+2(\alpha-1)})}
and

2^{J_{m}}\asymp m^{{1}/({d+2(\alpha-1)})}
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(M_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)+M_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)\Bigr),

(226)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigl(M_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)+M_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)\Bigr),

(227)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)\right|\right]&\leq C\Bigl(\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha)+\mathfrak{L}_{m}(\alpha)+M_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)+M_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)\Bigr).\end{aligned}

(228)

Appendix H
Proof of Theorem
32

H.1.
Convergence rates of wavelet density estimator

We derive an upper bound for the convergence rate of the wavelet density estimator. Throughout this subsection we write

\displaystyle s\coloneqq\alpha-1>0,

(229)

and we assume that the population densities satisfy

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-1}\leq p(x),q(x)\leq\Gamma,\qquad\|p\|_{C^{s}(\Omega)}+\|q\|_{C^{s}(\Omega)}\leq M,

(230)

for some
M,\Gamma>0
. We also define the rate templates

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha)\coloneqq\begin{cases}n^{-1},&d=1,\\
(\log n)^{2}n^{-1},&d=2,\\
n^{{-2\alpha/(2(\alpha-1)+d)}},&d\geq 3,\end{cases}

(231)

and the common
L^{1}
density-estimation rate

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha)\coloneqq n^{{-(\alpha-1)/(2(\alpha-1)+d)}}.

(232)

Theorem 33

(Wavelet density rates)
.

Assume
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
,
\alpha>1
, and
\alpha\notin\mathbb{N}
.
Assume moreover that

\displaystyle\Gamma^{-1}\leq p(x),q(x)\leq\Gamma,\qquad\|p\|_{C^{\alpha-1}(\Omega)}+\|q\|_{C^{\alpha-1}(\Omega)}\leq M,

that the boundary-corrected wavelet basis in Definition
3
has regularity strictly greater than
\alpha-1
, and choose

\displaystyle 2^{J_{n}}\asymp n^{{1/(d+2(\alpha-1))}},\qquad 2^{J_{m}}\asymp m^{{1/(d+2(\alpha-1))}}.

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
d,\alpha,M,\Gamma
and the chosen basis, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq CM_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha),

(233)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq CM_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha),

(234)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha),

(235)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-q\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathfrak{L}_{m}(\alpha).

(236)

Proof of Theorem
33
.

We first prove the
L^{1}
bounds.

Wavelet
L^{1}
error.

Let

\displaystyle p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}(x)\coloneqq\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}\alpha_{\zeta}\zeta(x)+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\sum_{\xi\in\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}}\alpha_{\xi}\xi(x),

where
\alpha_{\zeta}=\int_{\Omega}\zeta(x)p(x)dx
and
\alpha_{\xi}=\int_{\Omega}\xi(x)p(x)dx
.
Let

\displaystyle V_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\coloneqq\mathrm{span}(\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\mathrm{span}(\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}),

so that
p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}
is exactly the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
p
onto
V_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}
.
Because the chosen basis is the Cohen-Daubechies-Vial boundary-corrected basis from Definition
3
, has regularity strictly larger than
s=\alpha-1
, and
\alpha\notin\mathbb{N}
implies
C^{s}(\Omega)=\mathcal{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\Omega)
with equivalent norms, the bias bound established in the proof of
[
24
, Lemma 30]
applies to the boundary-corrected projection
p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}=P_{V_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}}p
and gives

\displaystyle\|p-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}=\|p-P_{V_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}}p\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C2^{-J_{n}s},

and therefore also

\displaystyle\|p-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C2^{-J_{n}s}.

For the stochastic part, orthonormality and Parseval imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\alpha}_{\zeta})+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\sum_{\xi\in\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}}\mathrm{Var}(\widehat{\alpha}_{\xi})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\left(\#\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\#\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{n}\left(2^{dj_{0}}+(2^{d}-1)\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}2^{dj}\right)\leq C\frac{2^{dJ_{n}}}{n}.

Since
|\Omega|=1
,
\|f\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\leq\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
; hence by Jensen,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\bigr]\right)^{1/2}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{2^{dJ_{n}}}{n}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq C\left(2^{-J_{n}s}+\sqrt{\frac{2^{dJ_{n}}}{n}}\right).

Balancing the two terms with
2^{J_{n}}\asymp n^{1/(d+2s)}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq Cn^{{-s/(2s+d)}}=C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha).

By Definition
3
, the scaling space
\mathrm{span}(\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}})
contains the constants, so there exist coefficients
(c_{\zeta})_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}
such that
1=\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}c_{\zeta}\zeta
. Hence

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}c_{\zeta}\widehat{\alpha}_{\zeta}={1/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}c_{\zeta}\zeta(X_{i})={1/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}1=1.

The same positive-part renormalization argument as in the proof of Proposition
29
(yielding (
186
)) therefore gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq 3\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha).

The same argument gives the target bound for
\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}
.

Wasserstein error.

The probability measures induced by
\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}
are exactly the boundary-corrected one-sample wavelet estimators studied in
[
24
, Lemma 30]
, whose Wasserstein bound yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx,pdx)\bigr]\leq C\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha),\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx,qdx)\bigr]\leq C\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha).

Finally, since the source and target masses are deterministic,

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)=M_{\mu}W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx,pdx),\qquad W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)=M_{\nu}W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx,qdx),

which proves (
233
)-(
236
).
∎

Lemma 34

(High-probability lower bound for the wavelet density)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
33
, write
\underline{p}\coloneqq\inf_{x\in\Omega}p(x)>0
and
\underline{q}\coloneqq\inf_{y\in\Omega}q(y)>0
, and define the event
