\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\coloneqq\left\{\inf_{x\in\Omega}\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\geq\frac{\underline{p}}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\inf_{y\in\Omega}\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(y)\geq\frac{\underline{q}}{2}\right\}.

Then for every
A>0
there exists
C_{A}>0
such that, for all sufficiently large
n,m
,

\displaystyle\Pr\bigl(\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav},c}\bigr)\leq C_{A}\bigl(n^{-A}+m^{-A}\bigr).

(237)

On
\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
, the positive-part renormalization is inactive, so
\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}=\widetilde{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}
, and the source and target densities are dominated by the wavelet fits:

\displaystyle d\mu\leq\frac{2\Gamma}{\underline{p}}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad d\nu\leq\frac{2\Gamma}{\underline{q}}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

(238)

Proof of Lemma
34
.

Let

\displaystyle K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,z)\coloneqq\sum_{\zeta\in\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}}\zeta(x)\zeta(z)+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\sum_{\xi\in\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}}\xi(x)\xi(z),

so that
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)=\tfrac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,X_{i})
. Orthonormality and the bound
\#\Phi^{\mathrm{bc}}+\sum_{j=j_{0}}^{J_{n}}\#\Psi_{j}^{\mathrm{bc}}\leq C2^{J_{n}d}
give

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\Omega}K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,x)\leq C2^{J_{n}d},

and Cauchy-Schwarz in the wavelet expansion yields
|K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,z)|\leq K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,x)^{1/2}K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(z,z)^{1/2}\leq C2^{J_{n}d}
. Since
\{K_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,\cdot):x\in\Omega\}
is contained in
V_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}
, a subspace of
L^{2}(\Omega)
of dimension at most
C2^{J_{n}d}
, the same chaining and Bernstein argument used to establish (
189
) in the proof of Proposition
29
yields

\displaystyle\Pr\left(\|\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}>\frac{\underline{p}}{4}\right)\leq C_{A}n^{-A}

for every
A>0
and all sufficiently large
n
. Since
\|p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}-p\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C2^{-J_{n}s}\to 0
, the same conclusion holds for
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p
in place of
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p_{J_{n}}^{\mathrm{bc}}
. The target bound is identical, and the union bound proves (
237
).

On
\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
,
\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}\geq\underline{p}/2>0
pointwise, so the positive part is the function itself and
\int_{\Omega}(\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}})_{+}=\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=1
, giving
\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=\widetilde{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
. Hence
\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=M_{\mu}\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx\geq M_{\mu}(\underline{p}/2)dx
, while
\mu=M_{\mu}pdx\leq M_{\mu}\Gamma dx
, which combine to give the source bound in (
238
); the target bound is identical.
∎

H.2.
Proof of the upper bound

Throughout this section we work on the Euclidean domain
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
and write

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}

for the boundary-corrected wavelet plugin objects from Definition
4
.

Lemma 35

(Plug-in UOT excess identity)
.

Work on
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, and let

\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}

be the boundary-corrected wavelet plugin objects from Definition
4
.
Let
(\varphi_{0},\psi_{0})
be an optimal dual pair for
\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)
, define

\displaystyle\zeta_{0}(x)\coloneqq-\bigl(e^{-\varphi_{0}(x)}-1\bigr),\qquad\xi_{0}(y)\coloneqq-\bigl(e^{-\psi_{0}(y)}-1\bigr),

and let
\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
be any optimal plan for
\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
with marginals
(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})
.
Define the oracle active fitted marginals by

\displaystyle\gamma_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav},\mathrm{or}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},

\displaystyle\gamma_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{wav},\mathrm{or}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq e^{-\psi_{0}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

Then

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\int\zeta_{0}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\int\xi_{0}d\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}

(239)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega\times\Omega}\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}-\varphi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(y)\Bigr)d\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x,y)

\displaystyle+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid\gamma_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav},\mathrm{or}})+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid\gamma_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{wav},\mathrm{or}}).

In particular,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu)\geq 0.

(240)

Proof of Lemma
35
.

The derivation of (
239
) is identical to that of (
200
) in the proof of Corollary
31
, with
(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{ker}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{ker}},\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{ker}})
replaced by
(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})
: it relies only on optimality of
\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
for
\mathrm{UOT}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}})
and the rewriting of the source and target
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
terms with reference measures
e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
e^{-\psi_{0}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}
, neither of which depends on the specific construction of the fitted measures.

Finally, duality gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)=\int\zeta_{0}d\mu+\int\xi_{0}d\nu,

and (
240
) follows from the nonnegativity of the dual slack and of the
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
terms.
∎

Theorem 36

(Plug-in UOT stability bound)
.

Assume Assumptions
1
and
3
, and work on
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
.
Then

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu)

(241)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

In particular,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)\right|

\displaystyle\leq M_{\mu}\|\zeta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}

(242)

\displaystyle+M_{\nu}\|\xi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-q\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

Proof of Theorem
36
.

The lower bound in (
241
) is exactly (
240
) from Lemma
35
.
Since
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
is compact, convex, and satisfies the interior cone condition, Proposition
7
applies to the pair
(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}})
, which has the same masses
M_{\mu}
and
M_{\nu}
as
(\mu,\nu)
. This gives the upper bound in (
241
).

For (
242
), write

\displaystyle A_{n,m}^{\mathrm{wav}}\coloneqq\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)+\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu).

By (
241
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)=A_{n,m}^{\mathrm{wav}}+R_{n,m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad 0\leq R_{n,m}^{\mathrm{wav}}\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

Therefore

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)\right|\leq|A_{n,m}^{\mathrm{wav}}|+C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

Since
\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=M_{\mu}\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}dx
and
\mu=M_{\mu}pdx
,

\displaystyle\left|\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)\right|

\displaystyle=M_{\mu}\left|\int_{\Omega}\zeta_{0}(x)(\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-p(x))dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq M_{\mu}\|\zeta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}.

The target term is treated in the same way, giving (
242
).
∎

Theorem 37

(Plug-in transport map risk)
.

Assume Assumptions
1
and
3
, and work on
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
.
Suppose that the fitted problem between
(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}})
is Monge-type, with optimal plan

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}=(\mathrm{id},\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})_{\#}\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}=(\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=e^{-\widehat{\varphi}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{2}\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)

(243)

\displaystyle-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\leq\frac{2C_{\Lambda}}{\kappa}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

(244)

Moreover, the active-mass mismatch is controlled by the same remainder:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}})+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid e^{-\psi_{0}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}})\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr),

(245)

and the active-source factor satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\leq{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}).

(246)

Finally, there exists a constant
C_{\mathrm{tr}}>0
, depending only on
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}
and
\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)
, such that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}(2/\kappa+1)C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

(247)

Proof of Theorem
37
.

By Lemma
35
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu)\\
&\qquad=\int\Bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\|^{2}-\varphi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x))\Bigr)d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\\
&\qquad\quad+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}})+{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{1,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid e^{-\psi_{0}}\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}).\end{aligned}

(248)

Lemma
2
gives

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\|^{2}-\varphi_{0}(x)-\psi_{0}(\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x))\geq\frac{\kappa}{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

for all
x\in\Omega
.
Substituting this lower bound into (
248
) and discarding the nonnegative
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
terms yields the left inequality in (
243
). The right inequality in (
243
) is exactly Theorem
36
. Hence (
244
) follows immediately.
Keeping the
D_{\mathrm{KL}}
terms in (
248
) and using once again the upper bound from Theorem
36
proves (
245
).

For (
246
), the active-source identities
\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}=(\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}=a_{0}^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
allow us to apply Lemma
21
pointwise with
a=(\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})^{2}
and
b=a_{0}^{2}
; integrating against
\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\leq{D_{\mathrm{KL}}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\mid e^{-\varphi_{0}}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}).

To prove (
247
), we transfer the map error from the active marginal to
\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
. Since
\varphi_{0}
is continuous and
\Omega
is compact, there exists a constant
w_{-}>0
such that
a_{0}(x)^{2}=e^{-\varphi_{0}(x)}\geq w_{-}
for all
x\in\Omega
. Because
\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
T_{0}
both take values in
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, we also have
\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)^{2}=d
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{w_{-}}a_{0}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{w_{-}}\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{w_{-}}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)^{2}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}+C_{\mathrm{tr}}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-a_{0}(x)|^{2},

for a constant
C_{\mathrm{tr}}>0
depending only on
w_{-}
and
\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)
. Integrating against
d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and using
d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}=\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)
together with (
244
), (
245
), and (
246
) yields (
247
).
∎

Corollary 38

(Plug-in growth map risk)
.

Assume that
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, that Assumptions
1
and
3
hold.
Let

\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}

be the boundary-corrected wavelet plugin objects from Definition
4
. Suppose that the fitted problem between
(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}})
is Monge-type, with optimal plan

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}=(\mathrm{id},\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})_{\#}\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}=(\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr).

(249)

Proof of Corollary
38
.

By Lemma
23
applied with
\widehat{\eta}=\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}
,
\widehat{a}=\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
,
\widehat{T}=\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
, and
\widehat{\gamma}_{0}=\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\lambda_{0}|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}\leq C\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-a_{0}|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}+C\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-T_{0}\|^{2}d\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

The first term on the right is bounded by combining (
246
) with (
245
), and the second is bounded by (
244
).
∎

Corollary 39

(Wavelet plugin rate transfer)
.

Assume that
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, that
c(x,y)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}
, and that Assumptions
1
and
3
hold, together with the smoothness assumptions of Theorem
33
so that the high-probability event
\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
of Lemma
34
is available.
Let

\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}},\ \widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}

be the boundary-corrected wavelet plugin objects from Definition
4
, and write

\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}_{0,nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}\coloneqq(\widehat{a}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}})^{2}\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

Suppose that the fitted problem between
(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}})
is Monge-type.
Assume that for some deterministic sequences
r_{n,\mathrm{wav}},r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}\geq 0
, where
r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}\geq 1/n
and
r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}\geq 1/m
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)\bigr]\leq r_{n,\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\bigr]\leq r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}.

Then there exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on the constants in Assumptions
1
and
3
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\right]\leq C(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}),

(250)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)\right]\leq C(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}),

(251)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu)\right|\right]\leq C_{\Lambda}(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}).

(252)

If, in addition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{p}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-p\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq\ell_{n,\mathrm{wav}},\qquad\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\widehat{q}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-q\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\bigr]\leq\ell_{m,\mathrm{wav}},

then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}\left[\left|\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)\right|\right]&\leq M_{\mu}\|\zeta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\ell_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+M_{\nu}\|\xi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\ell_{m,\mathrm{wav}}\\
&\quad+C_{\Lambda}(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}).\end{aligned}

(253)

Proof of Corollary
39
.

We first prove (
250
). On the high-probability event
\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
of Lemma
34
, the density-domination relation (
238
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\leq\frac{2\Gamma}{\underline{p}}\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x).

Taking expectations in this inequality on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and applying (
247
) together with the assumed Wasserstein bounds yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}}\right]\leq C(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}).

On the complement, both
\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
and
T_{0}
take values in
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
, so
\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}\leq d
, and (
237
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Omega}\|\widehat{T}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav},c}}\right]\leq dM_{\mu}\Pr(\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav},c})\leq C_{A}(n^{-A}+m^{-A}).

Choosing
A\geq 2
, this contribution is
O(n^{-2}+m^{-2})
and is absorbed by
C(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}})
, proving (
250
).

For the growth factor, the same density-domination on
\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
together with (
249
) gives

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)\leq\frac{2\Gamma}{\underline{p}}C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr)\quad\text{on }\mathcal{G}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}.

On the complement,
\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}
is clipped to
[w_{-},w_{+}]
and
\lambda_{0}
is bounded above and below on
\Omega
by Assumption
1
, so
|\widehat{\lambda}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\lambda_{0}|^{2}
is uniformly bounded; the same union-bound argument as above absorbs the bad-event contribution into
C(r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}+r_{m,\mathrm{wav}})
, proving (
251
).

For (
252
), (
241
) implies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{\mathrm{UOT}}_{nm}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mathrm{UOT}(\mu,\nu)-\int\zeta_{0}d(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\mu)-\int\xi_{0}d(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}-\nu)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\Lambda}\Bigl(W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}},\mu)+W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{\nu}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}},\nu)\Bigr),

so taking expectations gives the claim. Finally, taking expectations in (
242
) and using the assumed
L^{1}
bounds yields (
253
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
32
.

Apply Corollary
39
with

\displaystyle r_{n,\mathrm{wav}}=CM_{\mu}\mathfrak{R}_{n}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha),\quad r_{m,\mathrm{wav}}=CM_{\nu}\mathfrak{R}_{m}^{\mathrm{wav}}(\alpha),\quad\ell_{n,\mathrm{wav}}=C\mathfrak{L}_{n}(\alpha),\quad\ell_{m,\mathrm{wav}}=C\mathfrak{L}_{m}(\alpha),

as supplied by Theorem
33
. The constants
M_{\mu}\|\zeta_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
M_{\nu}\|\xi_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}
are absorbed into the generic constant
C
.
∎

Appendix I
Proof of Theorem
5

I.1.
Statistical model and lower-bound class

Let
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
. We assume that
M_{\mu},M_{\nu}
are known and observe two independent samples
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\bar{\mu}\coloneqq\mu/M_{\mu}
and
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{n}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\bar{\nu}\coloneqq\nu/M_{\nu}
. We write
\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu}^{n}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mu,\nu}^{n}
for the joint law and expectation. The KL divergence between two pairs of source and target measures
(\mu,\nu)
and
(\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu}^{n}\|\mathbb{P}_{\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}}^{n})=n\bigl[D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\mu}\|\bar{\mu}^{\prime})+D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}\|\bar{\nu}^{\prime})\bigr].

(254)

For the appendix proof, write
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}
for
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}(M,B,\Lambda)
from Section
4.5
. We introduce the shorthand minimax risks

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{T})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu,\nu}^{n}\left[\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\|\widehat{T}(x)-T_{0}(x)\|^{2}d\mu(x)\right],

(255)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{\lambda}(\widehat{\lambda})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{(\mu,\nu)\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}}\mathbb{E}_{\mu,\nu}^{n}\left[\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\widehat{\lambda}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)|^{2}d\mu(x)\right].

(256)

I.2.
Tools for minimax lower-bound

Throughout, we use standard tools for minimax lower bounds. See, e.g.,
[
36
, Theorem 2.2 and 2.5]
, restated here for convenience.

External result 40

(Lower bound from two hypotheses,
[
36
]
)
.

If probability measures
P_{0},P_{1}
satisfy
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{1}\|P_{0})\leq\alpha<\infty
and
d(\theta_{0},\theta_{1})\geq 2s
, then there exists a constant
c_{\alpha}>0
such that
\inf_{\widehat{\theta}}\max_{i=0,1}P_{i}(d(\widehat{\theta},\theta_{i})\geq s)\geq c_{\alpha}
.

External result 41

(Lower bound from multiple hypotheses,
[
36
]
)
.

Let
(\Theta,d)
be a pseudometric space and
\{P_{\theta}\}_{\theta\in\Theta}
a family of probability measures. Suppose there exist
\theta_{0},\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{K}\in\Theta
and
s>0
such that
P_{\theta_{k}}\ll P_{\theta_{0}}
for
k=1,\ldots,K
,
d(\theta_{k},\theta_{k^{\prime}})\geq 2s
for
0\leq k\neq k^{\prime}\leq K
, and
K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{\theta_{k}}\|P_{\theta_{0}})\leq(\log K)/9
, with
K\geq 2
. Then there exists a constant
c>0
such that
\inf_{\widehat{\theta}}\sup_{k}P_{\theta_{k}}(d(\widehat{\theta},\theta_{k})\geq s)\geq c
.

External result 42

(Varshamov-Gilbert bound,
[
36
, Lemma 2.9]
)
.

For every integer
N_{J}\geq 8
there exist binary strings
\omega^{(0)},\omega^{(1)},\dots,\omega^{(K)}\in\{0,1\}^{N_{J}}
with
\omega^{(0)}=\mathbf{0}
, cardinality
K\geq 2^{N_{J}/8}
, and pairwise Hamming separation

\displaystyle\|\omega^{(k)}-\omega^{(k^{\prime})}\|_{2}^{2}=\#\{i:\omega^{(k)}_{i}\neq\omega^{(k^{\prime})}_{i}\}\geq N_{J}/8\qquad\text{for all }0\leq k\neq k^{\prime}\leq K.

(257)

I.3.
Proof of Theorem
5

Proof.

To establish the minimax lower bound, we will appeal to both External result
40
and
41
; both involve constructing a collection of hypotheses
(\mu,\nu_{k})\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}
for which estimation is hard. Throughout,
O_{\infty}(r_{m})
denotes a function
f:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
with
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq Cr_{m}
, where
C
is a constant independent of
k
and
J
. For any matrix-valued function
A:[0,1]^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we denote
\|A\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\coloneqq\sup_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\|A(x)\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.

We start off by fixing
\mu\coloneqq\mathrm{Unif}([0,1]^{d})
throughout the entire proof, so that
d\mu(x)=dx
on
[0,1]^{d}
. All change-of-variables and integration-by-parts identities below are stated relative to the Lebesgue measure.

Nonparametric lower bound for
T_{0}
via balanced optimal transport.

The class
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}
contains a balanced OT subclass in which
\lambda_{0}\equiv 1
,
M_{\mu}=M_{\nu}=1
, and the Monge problem (
2
) reduces to the balanced OT problem on
[0,1]^{d}
. The minimax lower bound
n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha-2+d)}
for smooth transport map estimation in balanced OT was established in
[
21
]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{T}}\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{T})\gtrsim n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha-2+d)}.

(258)

Nonparametric lower bound for
\lambda_{0}
by reduction to balanced optimal transport.

We show that estimating the growth map
\lambda_{0}
is at least as hard as estimating a balanced OT transport map of smoothness
\alpha
, yielding the same nonparametric rate. The key observation is that
\lambda_{0}=\exp(-z+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla z\|^{2})
inherits
\alpha
-smoothness from
\|\nabla z\|^{2}
.

Let
\xi\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
be a non-zero bump with
\mathrm{supp}(\xi)\subset[0,1]
,
\xi(0)=\xi(1)=0
, and
\xi(x_{*})\neq 0
,
\xi^{\prime}(x_{*})\neq 0
for some
x_{*}\in(0,1)
; define

\displaystyle g(x)=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\xi(x_{i}),\qquad x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d}).

(259)

Fix a small constant
a>0
and a smooth cutoff
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,1)^{d})
with
\chi\equiv 1
on
[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
for some
\delta_{0}\in(0,1/4)
. Define

\displaystyle\varphi_{0}(x)\coloneqq a\chi(x)x_{1}.

(260)

Since
\varphi_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,1)^{d})
, the induced pair
(T_{0},\lambda_{0})
via (
6
) satisfies
T_{0}=\mathrm{id}
and
\lambda_{0}=1
on a neighborhood of
\partial[0,1]^{d}
. For
a
small enough, the function
\Psi_{0}(x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}-\varphi_{0}
remains strictly convex (since
\|\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\leq Ca
), and
T_{0}:[0,1]^{d}\to[0,1]^{d}
is a diffeomorphism. This construction yields
\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)=(a,0,\dots,0)
for all
x\in[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
.

Let
J=\lceil\theta n^{1/(2\alpha-2+d)}\rceil
for a constant
\theta>0
to be determined later, and place a grid
\{x^{(j)}\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
with
x^{(j)}_{i}=(j_{i}-1)/J
, with
\mathcal{J}\subset[J]^{d}
, satisfying
[x^{(j)},x^{(j)}+1/J]^{d}\subset[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
; the cardinality
N_{J}\coloneqq|\mathcal{J}|\asymp J^{d}
satisfies
c_{l}J^{d}\leq N_{J}\leq c_{u}J^{d}
for some constants
c_{l},c_{u}>0
. Define

\displaystyle g_{j}(x)\coloneqq\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}}{J^{\alpha+1}}g(J(x-x^{(j)})),

(261)

so that
\{\mathrm{supp}(g_{j})\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
are pairwise disjoint and contained in
[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
. Since
\partial^{\flat}g_{j}(\cdot)=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}J^{|\flat|-\alpha-1}\partial^{\flat}g(J(\cdot-x^{(j)}))
for any multi-index
\flat
, and
\alpha>1
, one may fix
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}
small enough and
J\geq J_{0}
large enough that uniformly in
j,J
:

\displaystyle\|g_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq J^{-\alpha-1},\quad\|\nabla g_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq J^{-\alpha},\quad\|\nabla^{2}g_{j}\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\leq 1/2,\quad\|g_{j}\|_{C^{\alpha+1}}\leq C_{g},

(262)

for some constant
C_{g}>0
. Applying External result
42
with
N_{J}\geq 8
, we obtain binary strings
\omega^{(0)},\dots,\omega^{(K)}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathcal{J}}
with
\omega^{(0)}=\mathbf{0}
,
K\geq 2^{N_{J}/8}
, and
\|\omega^{(k)}-\omega^{(k^{\prime})}\|_{2}^{2}\geq N_{J}/8
for
k\neq k^{\prime}
. Define

\displaystyle\varphi_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\varphi_{0}(x)+\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\omega^{(k)}_{j}g_{j}(x),\qquad k=0,\dots,K,

(263)

\displaystyle T_{k}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq x-\nabla\varphi_{k}(x),\quad\lambda_{k}(x)\coloneqq\exp\Big(-\varphi_{k}(x)+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}(x)\|^{2}\Big),

(264)

\displaystyle\nu_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(T_{k})_{\#}(\lambda_{k}^{2}\mu).

(265)

Write
\Psi_{k}(x)\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}-\varphi_{k}(x)
, so that
T_{k}=\nabla\Psi_{k}
. Since
\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}=\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}+\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})
and
\|\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\leq Ca
,
\|\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\leq 1/2
by (
262
), we have
\nabla^{2}\Psi_{k}\succeq(1/2-Ca)I_{d}\succ 0
for
a
small enough, so
\Psi_{k}
is strictly convex and
\varphi_{k}
is
c
-concave. It then follows from Theorem
1
that
(T_{k},\lambda_{k})
is the unique solution of the Monge problem (
2
) associated with
\varphi_{k}
for the pair
(\mu,\nu_{k})
. Consequently,
(T_{k},\lambda_{k})
is uniquely associated with
(\mu,\nu_{k})
.

Moreover,
\varphi_{k}\in C^{\alpha+1}(\Omega)
with
\|\varphi_{k}\|_{C^{\alpha+1}(\Omega)}\leq\|\varphi_{0}\|_{C^{\alpha+1}}+C_{g}
, so
T_{k}\in C^{\alpha}(\Omega;\Omega)
and
\lambda_{k}\in C^{\alpha}(\Omega)
with uniform bounds. Because
\varphi_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}((0,1)^{d})
and
w_{k}\coloneqq\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}
is supported strictly inside
[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
, we have
\nabla\varphi_{k}\equiv 0
on a neighborhood of
\partial[0,1]^{d}
, hence
T_{k}\equiv\mathrm{id}
there and
T_{k}([0,1]^{d})\subset[0,1]^{d}
.

Because
\mu=\mathrm{Unif}([0,1]^{d})
and each perturbation is supported strictly inside
[0,1]^{d}
, the induced target densities remain uniformly positive, uniformly bounded, and equal to
1
near the boundary. Together with the uniform
C^{\alpha}
bounds on
T_{k}
and
\lambda_{k}
, this shows that, after fixing
B
and
\Lambda
large enough, each pair
(\mu,\nu_{k})
belongs to
\mathcal{U}_{\alpha}
. In particular, the lower-bound construction lives in the same regime as the upper bound for the kernel-based method (Theorem
4
).

To utilize the multiple hypotheses in External result
41
, we first calculate the
L^{2}(\mu)
-separation between
\lambda_{k}
’s. Denote
\ell_{0}\coloneqq\log\lambda_{0}=-\varphi_{0}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\|^{2}
and
\ell_{k}\coloneqq\log\lambda_{k}=-\varphi_{k}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|^{2}
. Since
\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)=(a,0,\dots,0)
for all
x\in[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
, we compute

\displaystyle\ell_{k}-\ell_{0}

\displaystyle=\bigl(-\varphi_{k}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|^{2}\bigr)-\bigl(-\varphi_{0}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\|^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=-(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\varphi_{0}\cdot\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|^{2}

(266)

\displaystyle=\frac{a}{2}\partial_{1}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})-(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|^{2}.

(267)

The three terms have pointwise sizes
O(aJ^{-\alpha})
,
O(J^{-\alpha-1})
, and
O(J^{-2\alpha})
; since
a
is a fixed constant, the cross term
{(a/2)}\partial_{1}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})
dominates for
J
large. By
\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{0}(e^{\ell_{k}-\ell_{0}}-1)
and
|e^{u}-1-u|\leq u^{2}e^{|u|}
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{0}\Big(\frac{a}{2}\partial_{1}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})+R_{k}\Big),

(268)

where
|R_{k}|\leq|(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})|+C\|\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|^{2}+C^{\prime}(\ell_{k}-\ell_{0})^{2}\leq C^{\prime\prime}J^{-\alpha-1}
for
J\geq J_{0}
.

We now consider
\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k^{\prime}}
for
k\neq k^{\prime}
. Setting
\lambda_{\min}\coloneqq\inf_{x\in[0,1]^{d}}\lambda_{0}(x)>0
, we use the leading
\partial_{1}
-cross term in (
268
) together with the disjointness of
\{\mathrm{supp}(\nabla g_{j})\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
. We claim

\displaystyle c_{g}\coloneqq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}|\partial_{1}g(y)|^{2}dy>0.

(269)

Indeed,
\partial_{1}g
is the partial derivative of the bump
g(x)=\prod_{i}\xi(x_{i})
in the first coordinate. If
\partial_{1}g\equiv 0
on
[0,1]^{d}
, then
g
would be constant in
x_{1}
; combined with
\mathrm{supp}(g)\subset[0,1]^{d}
and
\xi(0)=\xi(1)=0
, this would force
g(x_{*})=0
, contradicting
\xi^{\prime}(x_{*})\neq 0
. Hence
\partial_{1}g
is non-zero on a set of positive Lebesgue measure, giving (
269
). By the rescaling
\partial_{1}g_{j}(x)=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}J^{-\alpha}(\partial_{1}g)(J(x-x^{(j)}))
and the substitution
y=J(x-x^{(j)})
,

\displaystyle\int|\partial_{1}g_{j}|^{2}d\mu=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}c_{g}J^{-2\alpha-d}.

(270)

By (
268
), the disjoint supports of
\{\partial_{1}g_{j}\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
, and
\|\omega^{(k)}-\omega^{(k^{\prime})}\|_{2}^{2}\geq N_{J}/8\geq c_{l}J^{d}/8
, applying the inequality
\|A+B\|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\|A\|^{2}-\|B\|^{2}
with
A=\lambda_{0}(a/2)\partial_{1}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{k^{\prime}})
and
B=\lambda_{0}(R_{k}-R_{k^{\prime}})
yields

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{k}-\lambda_{k^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{a^{2}\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{8}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}(\omega_{j}^{(k)}-\omega_{j}^{(k^{\prime})})^{2}\int|\partial_{1}g_{j}|^{2}d\mu-\lambda_{\max}^{2}\int(R_{k}-R_{k^{\prime}})^{2}d\mu

\displaystyle\geq\frac{a^{2}\lambda_{\min}^{2}}{8}\cdot\frac{c_{l}J^{d}}{8}\cdot\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}c_{g}J^{-2\alpha-d}-CJ^{-2\alpha-2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{\lambda}J^{-2\alpha},

(271)

for
J
large enough that the remainder
CJ^{-2\alpha-2}
is absorbed; the constant
c_{\lambda}\coloneqq(1/128)a^{2}\lambda_{\min}^{2}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}c_{g}c_{l}>0
is independent of
J,k,k^{\prime}
.

Next, we bound the KL divergence
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{k}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})
between the normalized hypotheses
\bar{\nu}_{k}\coloneqq\nu_{k}/\nu_{k}(\Omega)
. Throughout, we denote
h_{k}\coloneqq d\nu_{k}/d\mu
.

Write
M_{k}\coloneqq\nu_{k}(\Omega)=\int h_{k}d\mu
, so that the density of
\bar{\nu}_{k}
with respect to
\mu
is
\bar{h}_{k}\coloneqq h_{k}/M_{k}
. Since
\nu_{k}=(T_{k})_{\#}(\lambda_{k}^{2}\mu)
,
M_{k}=\int\lambda_{k}^{2}d\mu
, and because
\|\varphi_{k}\|_{\infty},\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|_{\infty}
are bounded uniformly in
k
, the weight
\lambda_{k}=\exp(-\varphi_{k}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|^{2})
satisfies
0<\lambda_{\min}\leq\lambda_{k}\leq\lambda_{\max}<\infty
uniformly in
k
. Combined with
\mu(\Omega)=1
, this yields

\displaystyle\lambda_{\min}^{2}\leq M_{k}\leq\lambda_{\max}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }k=0,\dots,K,

(272)

We bound the KL-divergence by the
\chi^{2}
-divergence in two steps. First, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality applied to
(M_{k}-M_{0})=\int(h_{k}-h_{0})d\mu=\int\frac{h_{k}-h_{0}}{\sqrt{h_{0}}}\cdot\sqrt{h_{0}}d\mu
gives

\displaystyle(M_{k}-M_{0})^{2}\leq\Bigl(\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu\Bigr)\Bigl(\int h_{0}d\mu\Bigr)=M_{0}\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu.

(273)

Splitting
\bar{h}_{k}-\bar{h}_{0}=\frac{h_{k}-h_{0}}{M_{k}}-\frac{h_{0}(M_{k}-M_{0})}{M_{0}M_{k}}
, we have

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{k}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})\leq\int\frac{(\bar{h}_{k}-\bar{h}_{0})^{2}}{\bar{h}_{0}}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2M_{0}}{M_{k}^{2}}\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu+\frac{2(M_{k}-M_{0})^{2}}{M_{k}^{2}M_{0}}\int h_{0}d\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{2M_{0}}{M_{k}^{2}}\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu+\frac{2(M_{k}-M_{0})^{2}}{M_{k}^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:CS-mass}}}{{\leq}}\frac{4M_{0}}{M_{k}^{2}}\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu\leq C\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu,

(274)

where the last inequality uses
\lambda_{\min}^{2}\leq M_{k}\leq\lambda_{\max}^{2}
to bound
4M_{0}/M_{k}^{2}\leq 4\lambda_{\max}^{2}/\lambda_{\min}^{4}=\colon C
, a constant depending only on
\lambda_{\min}
and
\lambda_{\max}
. To bound the integral, we consider
r_{k}(y)\coloneqq h_{k}(y)/h_{0}(y)
. If
\|\log r_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/2
for a sufficiently large
J
, the inequality
|e^{u}-1-u|\leq u^{2}
for
|u|\leq 1/2
applied to
u=\log r_{k}(y)
and multiplied by
h_{0}(y)
gives:

\displaystyle h_{k}(y)-h_{0}(y)=h_{0}(y)\log r_{k}(y)+R_{k}(y),\qquad|R_{k}(y)|\leq h_{0}(y)(\log r_{k}(y))^{2}.

(275)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\int\frac{(h_{k}-h_{0})^{2}}{h_{0}}d\mu\leq\|h_{0}\|_{\infty}\int(\log r_{k})^{2}d\mu.

(276)

It thus suffices to bound
\|\log r_{k}\|_{\infty}
. By the change-of-variables formula for
\nu_{k}=(T_{k})_{\#}(\lambda_{k}^{2}\mu)
,
\lambda_{k}=\exp(-\varphi_{k}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|^{2})
, and
\nabla T_{k}=I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}
,

\displaystyle\log h_{k}(y)

\displaystyle=\Bigl[2\log\lambda_{k}(x)-\log\det\nabla T_{k}(x)\Bigr]\Big|_{x=T_{k}^{-1}(y)}

(277)

\displaystyle=\Bigl[\underbrace{-2\varphi_{k}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}(x)\|^{2}-\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}(x))}_{L_{k}(x)}\Bigr]\Big|_{x=T_{k}^{-1}(y)}.

(278)

Thus
\log r_{k}(y)=L_{k}(T_{k}^{-1}(y))-L_{0}(T_{0}^{-1}(y))
, which we split as

\displaystyle\log r_{k}(y)=(L_{k}-L_{0})(T_{k}^{-1}(y))+\bigl[L_{0}(T_{k}^{-1}(y))-L_{0}(T_{0}^{-1}(y))\bigr].

(279)

We first bound
L_{k}(x)-L_{0}(x)
. By definition,

\displaystyle L_{k}(x)-L_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=-2(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})(x)+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\|\nabla\varphi_{k}(x)\|^{2}-\|\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)\|^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigl[-\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}(x))+\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}(x))\bigr].

(280)

We bound each term separately.

(i)

Zeroth-order term.
From the bounds following (
267
),
\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}=O_{\infty}(J^{-\alpha-1})
.

(ii)

Gradient-squared term.
Since
\nabla\varphi_{0}=(a,0,\dots,0)
on
[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi_{k}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\|^{2}=a\partial_{1}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|^{2}=O_{\infty}(aJ^{-\alpha})+O_{\infty}(J^{-2\alpha}).

(281)

(iii)

Log-determinant term.
We claim that pointwise on
\Omega
,

\displaystyle-\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}(x))+\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}(x))=-\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})(x)).

(282)

To see this, split
\Omega
into the bump supports and their complement. On each bump support
\mathrm{supp}(g_{j})\subset[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
,
\chi\equiv 1
, so
\varphi_{0}(x)=ax_{1}
is affine and
\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}(x)\equiv 0
; hence
\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}=\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})
and
\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0})=0
, which gives (
282
). Off the bump supports,
\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}=\sum_{j}\omega_{j}^{(k)}g_{j}=0
, so
\nabla^{2}\varphi_{k}=\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}
and both sides of (
282
) vanish. The claim thus follows.

Applying the Taylor expansion
\log\det(I-A)=-\mathrm{tr}(A)+O(\|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2})
with
A=\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})
, which is valid since
\|\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\leq 1/2
by (
262
), we have

\displaystyle-\log\det(I-\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}))=\Delta(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})+O_{\infty}(J^{2-2\alpha}),

(283)

where
O_{\infty}(J^{2-2\alpha})
follows from
\|\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}=O(J^{1-\alpha})
.

Combining (i)–(iii) with
\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}=O_{\infty}(J^{-\alpha-1})
, and noting that for
\alpha\geq 1
and
J\geq 1/a
the bound
J^{1-\alpha}\geq aJ^{-\alpha}\geq J^{-\alpha-1}\geq J^{-2\alpha}
lets us absorb lower-order terms,

\displaystyle L_{k}(x)-L_{0}(x)=\Delta\varphi_{k}(x)-\Delta\varphi_{0}(x)+O_{\infty}(aJ^{-\alpha})+O_{\infty}(J^{2-2\alpha}).

(284)

For the second term in (
279
), we have
L_{0}(x)=-2\varphi_{0}(x)+\frac{1}{2}a^{2}=-2ax_{1}+\mathrm{const}
on
[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
, so
\nabla L_{0}\equiv-2ae_{1}
and:

\displaystyle L_{0}(T_{k}^{-1}(y))-L_{0}(T_{0}^{-1}(y))=-2ae_{1}\cdot\bigl(T_{k}^{-1}(y)-T_{0}^{-1}(y)\bigr),

(285)

To bound the difference, we fix
y\in\Omega
and set
x_{k}\coloneqq T_{k}^{-1}(y)
,
x_{0}\coloneqq T_{0}^{-1}(y)
. From
y=T_{k}(x_{k})=x_{k}-\nabla\varphi_{k}(x_{k})
and
y=T_{0}(x_{0})=x_{0}-\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{0})
, we have

\displaystyle(x_{k}-x_{0})-\bigl(\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{k})-\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{0})\bigr)=\nabla\varphi_{k}(x_{k})-\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{k}).

(286)

By the fundamental theorem of calculus,
\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{k})-\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{0})=A_{y}(x_{k}-x_{0})
with
A_{y}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}(x_{0}+t(x_{k}-x_{0}))dt
and
\|A_{y}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\nabla^{2}\varphi_{0}\|_{\mathrm{op},\infty}\leq Ca
. Hence

\displaystyle(I-A_{y})(x_{k}-x_{0})=\nabla\varphi_{k}(x_{k})-\nabla\varphi_{0}(x_{k}).

(287)

For a sufficiently small
a>0
,
I-A_{y}
is invertible with
\|(I-A_{y})^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1-Ca)^{-1}
. Consequently, using
\|\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq J^{-\alpha}
from (
262
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\|T_{k}^{-1}(y)-T_{0}^{-1}(y)\|\leq\frac{\|\nabla(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|_{\infty}}{1-Ca}=O(J^{-\alpha}),

(288)

Substituting (
284
), (
285
) and (
288
) into (
279
) yields:

\displaystyle\log r_{k}(y)=\Delta\varphi_{k}(T_{k}^{-1}(y))-\Delta\varphi_{0}(T_{k}^{-1}(y))+O_{\infty}(aJ^{-\alpha})+O_{\infty}(J^{2-2\alpha}).

(289)

In particular
\|\log r_{k}\|_{\infty}=O(J^{1-\alpha})\leq 1/2
for a sufficiently large
J
, justifying the hypothesis used in (
275
).

Plugging the bound for
\log r_{k}
into (
276
), yields:

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{k}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})\leq C\int(\Delta\varphi_{k}\circ T_{k}^{-1}-\Delta\varphi_{0}\circ T_{k}^{-1})^{2}d\mu+O(a^{2}J^{-2\alpha})+O(J^{4-4\alpha}).

(290)

We now bound the leading term
\int(\Delta(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\circ T_{k}^{-1})^{2}d\mu
. The change of variables
x=T_{k}^{-1}(y)
has Jacobian
\det\nabla T_{k}(x)=\det(I-\nabla^{2}(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}))=1+O(J^{1-\alpha})
, so
\|\Delta(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\circ T_{k}^{-1}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\leq C\|\Delta(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0})\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}
. Using disjoint supports of
\{g_{j}\}_{j\in\mathcal{J}}
,
(\omega_{j}^{(k)})^{2}=\omega_{j}^{(k)}\in\{0,1\}
, and the rescaling
\Delta g_{j}(x)=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}J^{1-\alpha}(\Delta g)(J(x-x^{(j)}))
,

\displaystyle\int(\Delta(\varphi_{k}-\varphi_{0}))^{2}d\mu=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\omega_{j}^{(k)}\cdot\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}}{J^{2\alpha-2+d}}\int(\Delta g)^{2}dy\leq C\varepsilon_{\mathrm{b}}^{2}J^{2-2\alpha},

(291)

using
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\omega_{j}^{(k)}\leq N_{J}\leq c_{u}J^{d}
. The two remainders in (
290
) contribute
O(a^{2}J^{-2\alpha})
and
O(J^{4-4\alpha})
, both dominated by
J^{2-2\alpha}
for
\alpha\geq 1
. Hence,

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{k}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})\leq CJ^{2-2\alpha}.

(292)

By (
254
) and
\mu
fixed across hypotheses,

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu_{k}}^{n}\|\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu_{0}}^{n})=nD_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{k}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})\leq C_{0}nJ^{2-2\alpha}.

(293)

From External result
42
and
N_{J}\geq c_{l}J^{d}
,
\log K\geq(N_{J}/8)\log 2\geq(c_{l}J^{d}\log 2)/8
, so

\displaystyle(\log K)/9\geq(c_{l}J^{d}\log 2)/72.

(294)

Using
J\geq\theta n^{1/(2\alpha-2+d)}
from the definition
J=\lceil\theta n^{1/(2\alpha-2+d)}\rceil
,

\displaystyle C_{0}nJ^{2-2\alpha}\leq(\log K)/9\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\theta^{2\alpha-2+d}\geq 72C_{0}/(c_{l}\log 2).

(295)

Therefore, by choosing

\displaystyle\theta>\left(72C_{0}/(c_{l}\log 2)\right)^{1/(2\alpha-2+d)},

(296)

we have
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu_{k}}^{n}\|\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu_{0}}^{n})\leq\log K/9
, as required by External result
41
.

Applying External result
41
with
d_{\lambda}(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})^{2}\coloneqq\|\lambda-\lambda^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}
and separation
\gtrsim J^{-2\alpha}
from (
I.3
) yields

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\lambda}}\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{\lambda}(\widehat{\lambda})\gtrsim J^{-2\alpha}\asymp n^{-2\alpha/(2\alpha-2+d)}.

(297)

Parametric lower bound from two hypotheses.

We show
\inf_{\widehat{T}}\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{T})\gtrsim n^{-1}
and
\inf_{\widehat{\lambda}}\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{\lambda}(\widehat{\lambda})\gtrsim n^{-1}
simultaneously. Retain
\varphi_{0}
as in (
260
) and
\mu=\mathrm{Unif}([0,1]^{d})
. For
\widetilde{\theta}>0
small, define

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(x)\coloneqq\varphi_{0}(x)+(\widetilde{\theta}/\sqrt{n})g(x),

(298)

and let
(T_{i},\lambda_{i},\nu_{i})
be the corresponding pairs and targets via (
6
),
i=0,1
. For
\widetilde{\theta}
small and
n
large,
\Psi_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\|\cdot\|^{2}-\varphi_{1}
remains strictly convex, so
\varphi_{1}
is
c
-concave. It then follows from Theorem
1
that
(T_{1},\lambda_{1})
is the unique solution of (
2
) associated with
\varphi_{1}
for the pair
(\mu,\nu_{1})
.

For the separation between the two transport maps, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{0}-T_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}=(\widetilde{\theta}^{2}/n)\int\|\nabla g\|^{2}d\mu\gtrsim 1/n.

(299)

For the growth maps, since
\nabla\varphi_{0}=(a,0,\dots,0)
on
\mathrm{supp}(g)\subset[\delta_{0},1-\delta_{0}]^{d}
, the same expansion as in (
268
) with
w=(\widetilde{\theta}/\sqrt{n})g
gives

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(x)-\lambda_{0}(x)=\lambda_{0}(x)\frac{\widetilde{\theta}}{\sqrt{n}}\bigl[(a/2)\partial_{1}g(x)-g(x)\bigr]+R(x),

(300)

with
\|R\|_{\infty}=O(n^{-1})
. Squaring and integrating against
d\mu=dx
,

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\theta}^{2}}{n}\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\bigl[(a/2)\partial_{1}g-g\bigr]^{2}dx-O(n^{-2}).

(301)

We claim

\displaystyle c_{g}^{\mathrm{par}}\coloneqq\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\bigl[(a/2)\partial_{1}g(y)-g(y)\bigr]^{2}dy>0.

(302)

Indeed, suppose for contradiction
(a/2)\partial_{1}g-g\equiv 0
on
[0,1]^{d}
. Along any line
t\mapsto(t,x_{2},\dots,x_{d})
with fixed
(x_{2},\dots,x_{d})\in[0,1]^{d-1}
, the function
\varphi(t)\coloneqq g(t,x_{2},\dots,x_{d})
would satisfy the linear ODE
(a/2)\varphi^{\prime}(t)=\varphi(t)
, so
\varphi(t)=\varphi(0)e^{2t/a}
wherever the line lies in
[0,1]
. Since
\varphi
vanishes outside
[0,1]
(as
\mathrm{supp}(g)\subset[0,1]^{d}
and
\xi(0)=\xi(1)=0
), continuity forces
\varphi(0)=0
, hence
\varphi\equiv 0
. Applying this to every such line and to
x_{*}
gives
g(x_{*})=0
, contradicting
g(x_{*})\neq 0
. Therefore
(a/2)\partial_{1}g-g
is non-zero on a set of positive Lebesgue measure, giving (
302
). Hence

\displaystyle\|\lambda_{0}-\lambda_{1}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\geq\lambda_{\min}^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\theta}^{2}}{n}c_{g}^{\mathrm{par}}-O(n^{-2})\gtrsim 1/n.

(303)

We now consider the KL divergence. The expansion (
290
) with
J=1
gives
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{1}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})\lesssim\widetilde{\theta}^{2}/n
, so
nD_{\mathrm{KL}}(\bar{\nu}_{1}\|\bar{\nu}_{0})\lesssim\widetilde{\theta}^{2}
. By External result
40
,

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{T}}\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{T}(\widehat{T})

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1/n,

(304)

\displaystyle\inf_{\widehat{\lambda}}\mathfrak{M}_{n}^{\lambda}(\widehat{\lambda})

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1/n.

(305)

Combining (
258
), (
297
), (
304
), and (
305
) yields Theorem
5
.
∎

Appendix J
Wasserstein convergence of the weighted empirical measure

We record here that the Wasserstein convergence of
\widetilde{\mu}_{n}
is an immediate reduction to the probability-measure case. In particular, the idea of multiplying the transport plan in the proof of
[
39
]
by
M_{\mu}
is correct. The only point to keep in mind is that the factor
M_{\mu}
appears at the level of
W_{p}^{p}
, while
W_{p}
itself scales like
M_{\mu}^{1/p}
.

Definition 5
.

Let
p\in[1,\infty)
and let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)
satisfy
\alpha(\Omega)=\beta(\Omega)<\infty
.
We define the
p
-Wasserstein distance between
\alpha
and
\beta
by

\displaystyle W_{p}(\alpha,\beta)\coloneqq\left(\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\alpha,\beta)}\int_{\Omega\times\Omega}\|x-y\|^{p}d\pi(x,y)\right)^{1/p},

(306)

where
\Pi(\alpha,\beta)
denotes the set of couplings of
\alpha
and
\beta
.
When
\alpha
and
\beta
are probability measures, this is the usual Wasserstein distance.

Lemma 43

(Scaling in the total mass)
.

Let
m>0
and let
\alpha,\beta
be probability measures on
\Omega
.
Then

\displaystyle W_{p}^{p}(m\alpha,m\beta)=mW_{p}^{p}(\alpha,\beta),\qquad W_{p}(m\alpha,m\beta)=m^{1/p}W_{p}(\alpha,\beta).

(307)

Proof.

The map
\pi\mapsto m\pi
is a bijection from
\Pi(\alpha,\beta)
onto
\Pi(m\alpha,m\beta)
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf_{\gamma\in\Pi(m\alpha,m\beta)}\int\|x-y\|^{p}d\gamma(x,y)=m\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\alpha,\beta)}\int\|x-y\|^{p}d\pi(x,y).

(308)

Taking the
p
th root yields the second identity.
∎

We introduce main ingredients for bounding the convergence rates of empirical measures in Wasserstein distances.

For a probability measure
\eta
on
\Omega
and
\tau\in[0,1)
, let
N_{\varepsilon}(S)
be the minimum number of closed balls of diameter
\varepsilon
needed to cover
S
, and define:

\displaystyle N_{\varepsilon}(\eta,\tau)\coloneqq\inf\{N_{\varepsilon}(S):S\subset\Omega,\ \eta(S)\geq 1-\tau\},\qquad d_{\varepsilon}(\eta,\tau)\coloneqq\frac{\log N_{\varepsilon}(\eta,\tau)}{-\log\varepsilon}.

(309)

We also set

\displaystyle d_{p}^{*}(\eta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\inf\left\{s>2p:\limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}d_{\varepsilon}\left(\eta,\varepsilon^{sp/(s-2p)}\right)\leq s\right\},

(310)

\displaystyle d_{*}(\eta)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lim_{\tau\downarrow 0}\liminf_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}d_{\varepsilon}(\eta,\tau).

(311)

These are the upper and lower Wasserstein dimensions introduced by
[
39
]
.

Proposition 44

(Weighted empirical measure: convergence and rates)
.

Let
M_{\mu}\in(0,\infty)
.
Let
X_{1},X_{2},\dots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{i.i.d.}}}{{\sim}}\bar{\mu}
, and define

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}_{n}\coloneqq{1/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{X_{i}},\qquad\widetilde{\mu}_{n}\coloneqq{M_{\mu}/n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{X_{i}}.

(312)

Then, the following hold:

(i)

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle W_{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})=(M_{\mu})^{1/p}W_{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu}_{n}).

(313)

In particular,
W_{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})\to 0
almost surely as
n\to\infty
.

(ii)

If
s>d_{p}^{*}(\bar{\mu})
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{p}^{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})]\lesssim M_{\mu}n^{-p/s}.

(314)

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})]\lesssim(M_{\mu})^{1/p}n^{-1/s}.

(315)

(iii)

If
t<d_{*}(\bar{\mu})
and
\nu_{n}\in\mathcal{M}_{+}(\Omega)
is any discrete measure with

\nu_{n}(\Omega)=M_{\mu}
and
|\mathrm{supp}(\nu_{n})|\leq n
, then

\displaystyle W_{p}(\mu,\nu_{n})\gtrsim(M_{\mu})^{1/p}n^{-1/t}.

(316)

In particular, the same lower bound holds for
\nu_{n}=\widetilde{\mu}_{n}
.

Proof.

The identity in (i) is exactly Lemma
43
with
\alpha=\bar{\mu}
and
\beta=\bar{\mu}_{n}
.
Because
\overline{\Omega}
is compact, the empirical measures satisfy
\bar{\mu}_{n}\Rightarrow\bar{\mu}
almost surely.
Fix
x_{0}\in\Omega
.
Then
x\mapsto\|x-x_{0}\|^{p}
is bounded and continuous on
\overline{\Omega}
, so along the same event

\displaystyle\int\|x-x_{0}\|^{p}d\bar{\mu}_{n}(x)\to\int\|x-x_{0}\|^{p}d\bar{\mu}(x).

Weak convergence together with convergence of the
p
th moments is equivalent to convergence in
W_{p}
, hence
W_{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu}_{n})\to 0
almost surely.
Applying Lemma
43
once more gives
W_{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})\to 0
almost surely.

For (ii), fix
s>d_{p}^{*}(\bar{\mu})
.
By definition of
d_{p}^{*}(\bar{\mu})
, the hypothesis of Proposition 5 in
[
39
]
is satisfied for the probability measure
\bar{\mu}
on the compact metric space
\overline{\Omega}
after a harmless rescaling to
\mathrm{diam}(\Omega)\leq 1
.
Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{p}^{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu}_{n})]\leq C_{1}n^{-p/s}+C_{2}n^{-1/2}.

Because
s>2p
, the second term is of smaller order and can be absorbed into the first for all sufficiently large
n
.
Using Lemma
43
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{p}^{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})]

\displaystyle=M_{\mu}\mathbb{E}[W_{p}^{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu}_{n})]

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{\mu}n^{-p/s},

which proves (
314
).
The displayed bound for
\mathbb{E}[W_{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})]
follows from Jensen’s inequality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W_{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})]\leq\mathbb{E}[W_{p}^{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})]^{1/p}\lesssim M_{\mu}^{1/p}n^{-1/s}.

For (iii), let
\bar{\nu}_{n}\coloneqq\nu_{n}/(M_{\mu})
.
Then
\bar{\nu}_{n}
is a probability measure supported on at most
n
points.
The lower-bound part of Theorem 1 of
[
39
]
, together with the sentence immediately following that theorem, implies that for every
t<d_{*}(\bar{\mu})
,

\displaystyle W_{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu}_{n})\gtrsim n^{-1/t}.

Applying Lemma
43
once again yields

\displaystyle W_{p}(\mu,\nu_{n})=(M_{\mu})^{1/p}W_{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\nu}_{n})\gtrsim(M_{\mu})^{1/p}n^{-1/t}.

Taking
\nu_{n}=\widetilde{\mu}_{n}
proves the last claim.
∎

Remark 5
.

If one follows the constructive proof of
[
39
]
more literally, every partial transport plan in their dyadic argument can indeed be multiplied by
M_{\mu}
.
Equivalently,

\displaystyle W_{p}^{p}(\mu,\widetilde{\mu}_{n})=(M_{\mu})W_{p}^{p}(\bar{\mu},\bar{\mu}_{n}),

(317)

so any bound proved at the level of
W_{p}^{p}
for the normalized empirical measure transfers verbatim after multiplying the right-hand side by
M_{\mu}
.

Appendix K
Details of the simulation

K.1.
Computational resources

The experiments were run on a Linux computing node with an 8-core Intel(R) Xeon(R) Gold 5222 processor and 755 GB of system memory. The experiments were run on a single NVIDIA Tesla V100-SXM2 GPU, utilizing 32 GB of VRAM.

K.2.
Simulation and implementation details

In our simulation, the source and target measures
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{M}_{+}([0,1]^{d})
have
1
-Hölder smooth densities
\propto\prod_{i=1}^{d}\lvert\sin\pi(x_{i}-c)\rvert
, where
c=0.3
for
\mu
and
c=0.7
for
\nu
. We scale the measures so that
\mu([0,1]^{d})=1
and
\nu([0,1]^{d})=2.5
. We generate source and target samples of size
n\in\{100,200,500,1000\}
over 10 random seeds. For PB-1NN and PB-Kernel, we solve the discrete UOT problem by framing it as non-negative regularized linear regression as described in
[
7
]
. For PI-kernel and PI-wavelet, we solve the optimization problem in (
16
) using the L-BFGS algorithm
[
23
]
over the discretized grid with resolution
128,64,32,32
for
d=1,2,3,4
, respectively. For the plan-based estimators, we use the UOT solver from the Python Optimal Transport library
[
15
]
. For the purpose of learning rate estimation, we first estimate the oracle number of kernel basis elements
L_{n}
and wavelet resolution levels
J_{n}
via cross-validation with
n=5000
, and then scale
L_{n}
and
J_{n}
for each
n\in\{100,200,500,1000\}
according to Theorem
3
and
32
, respectively. The cone program in the SSUOT estimator was solved using the CVXPY library
[
10
]
with MOSEK solver
[
1
]
.
